High-Speed Nanoscale Brain Imaging Uncovers Hidden Synapses

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Researchers dramatically accelerated electron tomography to map large volumes of brain tissue at 15-nanometer 3D resolution. The breakthrough captures fine neural structures missed by conventional connectomes, including rapid-fire electrical synapses and uncharacterized protrusions where neurons appear to “nibble” neighboring glial cells.

To understand how billions of neurons orchestrate thoughts, memories, and movement, neuroscientists construct connectomes, detailed wiring diagrams mapping how brain cells connect and communicate. While macro-scale MRI scans chart major white-matter highways and standard electron microscopy traces individual chemical synapses, scaling up to large tissue volumes has historically forced a difficult compromise: sacrificing nanoscale structural detail for imaging speed.

As a result, even the most ambitious brain wiring maps have routinely missed ultra-fine cellular architectures, including electrical synapses (also called gap junctions), microscopic channels that enable direct, near-instantaneous electrical communication between adjacent neurons.

Now, in a study published November 19 in Nature Methods, researchers at Harvard Medical School (HMS) and the University of California San Diego have overcome that bottleneck by supercharging electron tomography—a 3D imaging technique that reconstructs nanoscale architecture by tilting tissue samples and capturing views from multiple angles.

Led by co-senior authors Dr. Wei-Chung Allen Lee (associate professor of neurobiology at HMS and associate professor of neurology at Boston Children’s Hospital) and Dr. Mark Ellisman (UC San Diego), alongside co-first authors Dr. Aaron T. Kuan and Dr. Sébastien Phan, the team demonstrated that large blocks of brain tissue can be mapped in exquisite 3D nanoscale detail without prohibitive imaging times.

Conventional electron tomography is painstakingly slow because it typically requires capturing hundreds of tilted images across razor-thin tissue slices. By refining image acquisition and computational reconstruction, Lee, Ellisman, and colleagues discovered they could cut the required number of images by roughly 50-fold while imaging tissue sections 20 times thicker than standard electron microscopy protocols, preserving an extraordinary 3D resolution of 15 nanometers or finer.

“We can now see brain structures that we haven’t been able to see before in connectomics data,” explained Lee. “We hope this work will help us understand how nervous systems are built; what changes with age; and what goes awry in neurodevelopmental, cognitive, and neurodegenerative disorders.”

When the researchers applied their high-speed tomography pipeline to tissue from the mouse cerebellum, the 3D reconstructions revealed both long-sought neural connections and surprising cellular behaviors:

“We’re seeing uncharacterized structures, suggesting that neurons are ‘nibbling’ their neighboring cells,” Lee said.

In addition, the researchers traced delicate nerve fibers continuously through the thick 3D tissue block right down to their exact synaptic contact points with target neurons.

While scaling this accelerated tomography workflow to map an entire mammalian brain will require automated tissue-handling robotics, the breakthrough bridges a critical resolution gap in modern connectomics—equipping neuroscientists to examine how fine electrical and glial-neuronal connections form in early life and break down in brain disease.

Why do traditional brain wiring maps (connectomes) miss certain structures?

Mapping large volumes of brain tissue at high speed usually forces scientists to sacrifice ultra-fine resolution. As a result, critical microscopic features, such as tiny electrical synapses (gap junctions) that connect neurons directly, often blur out or go undetected in large-scale datasets.

How did researchers speed up nanoscale 3D brain imaging?

The team optimized electron tomography so that it requires about 50 times fewer multi-angle images on tissue slices 20 times thicker than usual, while still maintaining a 3D resolution of 15 nanometers or finer.

What surprising new structures did they see in the brain tissue?

Alongside hard-to-see electrical synapses in the mouse cerebellum, the researchers discovered tiny protrusions between neurons and neighboring support cells (glia), suggesting that neurons physically “nibble” or take up material from adjacent cells.

Funding: This work was supported by the National Institutes of Health (grants RF1MH129261, U01NS142013, and K99EB032217), with startup support to Kuan from the Yale School of Medicine Department of Neuroscience and Wu Tsai Institute.

Harvard University filed a patent application regarding GridTape (WO2017184621A1) on behalf of inventors, including Lee, and negotiated licensing agreements with interested partners. All other authors have no competing interests.

Scalable electron tomography for connectomics

We demonstrate limited-tilt, serial-section electron tomography (ET), which can non-destructively map brain circuits over large 3D volumes and provide high-resolution, supramolecular details within subvolumes of interest.

Here we show accelerated ET imaging of semi-thick sections ( >500 nm) from the mouse cerebellar cortex, with 3D resolution <15 nm and machine learning-based image augmentation, providing the capacity to resolve key features of neuronal circuits including chemical synapses, endocytic structures and gap junctions.

Furthermore, we systematically assess how imaging parameters affect image quality and speed to enable connectomic-scale projects.

What Eater Editors Are Buying for Black Friday

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Not all deals are created equal — but it’s worth scoring cult-fave ceramics, cool-LA-person food gifts, and more

If you buy something from a link, Vox Media may earn a commission. See our ethics statement.

We’re a far cry from the Black Friday traditions of yore. Instead of storming the gates of department stores at dawn for Salad Shooters and Cabbage Patch dolls, we now sit on our own couches to peruse the deals online — and there are lots of deals. Too many deals! But, not enough that are worthy to qualify as truly worthwhile Black Friday promotions. (Ten or 15 percent? Please.) For the most part, however, the digital age of Black Friday deal-hunting is about machete-chopping your way through the roughage of “meh” promotions.

There are exceptions: Black Friday is usually when brands who rarely run promotions release limited-time deals, and it’s the smartest time of year to buy countertop appliances and electronics. Our online-shopping experts have done the hunting for you.

Below, we’ve rounded up the best Black Friday kitchen and food deals that have already gone live, and which are actually worth your time. This is the moment to invest anything you’ve ever pined over from heritage cookware brands, including the big kahuna: Le Creuset. Le Creuset’s Dutch ovens and baking dishes are going to be on escalating promotions across various retailers, so snag the colorways you love most while they’re still available.

Oft-imitated but never equaled, KitchenAid’s stand mixers last decades and look damn good on a counter, with their streamlined mid-century design and array of shades. Black Friday is the time to make the splurge, as they’re on sale across Crate & Barrel, Williams Sonoma, Amazon, and other retailers. This porcelain-white 5-quart option is a classic iteration that will look good in every kitchen, and it’s currently 30 percent off.

Renowned for their intricate craftsmanship and ergonomic, lightweight design, Laguiole knives have been made in the same eponymous French village for hundreds of years. The discounts have already started rolling out; there’s a set of six wood-handled knives on sale at Wayfair, a carving set priced down at Overstock that’ll come in handy for serving meat at future holidays, and a cream-colored set on sale at Mark and Graham that comes with the option of custom engraving.

Los Angeles-based lifestyle brand Flamingo Estate is a major fave among Eater editors, ogled for luxe items like its beloved heirloom-tomato-scented candle and Hachiya persimmon vinegar. It’s having a rare sitewide sale this week, with 20 percent off everything, including the new Big Pasta Dinner set and Affogato set with vanilla olive oil and espresso salt. Truly a one-stop-shop for fancy food gifts.

My Eater colleague Bettina Makalintal got me into Jono Pandolfi a few years ago, and the small rose-colored plates I bought with her at their holiday sale have become my favorites for plating desserts. I’m not alone: The New Jersey-based ceramic studio has an impressive roster of restaurants that use its dinnerware, including Gramercy Tavern, Lilia, and restaurants at cool hotels like the Ojai Valley Inn; that’s probably because they’re durable, come in unique colorways, and dishwasher- and microwave-safe. These gems don’t go on sale often, so add them to your cart while you can.

Vue developer learning React: what carries over, what's new, 30-day plan

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Posted on Nov 24 • Originally published at switchbyskill.com

By the end of the month you’ll have touched every core React concept, built three progressively larger apps, and exercised the tooling that surrounds a production React codebase.

The shift to React introduces a new syntax (JSX) and a hook‑centric mental model, which is steeper than Vue’s optional Composition API but still manageable with daily practice.

Not immediately; React works perfectly with plain JavaScript. However, most modern React codebases adopt TypeScript, so consider adding it after you’re comfortable with the core concepts.

React does not have an exact equivalent, but you can approximate the experience with tools like Vite and MDX. Most teams prefer separate .jsx/.tsx and .css files for clarity.

Context is good for low‑frequency global data (theme, locale). For complex state with actions, mutations, and devtools, a dedicated store (Zustand, Jotai, Redux) is more appropriate.

South Korea Moves to Lift Its ICO Ban After Nearly a Decade

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

South Korea Moves to Lift Its ICO Ban After Nearly a Decade

South Korea is proposing to end its nine-year ban on initial coin offerings, a move aimed at pulling crypto fundraising back onshore, creating jobs and folding token sales into a regulated framework.

The country’s Financial Services Commission unveiled a draft bill, the Act on Digital Asset Industry Innovation and User Protection, on Tuesday that would reverse the prohibition imposed in September 2017, when regulators banned ICOs over fraud, speculation and investor losses.

If passed, it would let domestic projects issue and sell tokens to the public for the first time in nearly a decade.

You can now act on the news you read: Go long or short Bitcoin with perpetual futures, all from your Decrypt Money Account.

Under the proposal, any issuer would have to file a “digital asset white paper” with the FSC before selling tokens, detailing the project, how the raised funds will be used, how many tokens will be issued and the risks involved. Issuers and their executives would face civil damages, financial penalties and possible criminal prosecution for false or omitted information, a liability regime modeled on the disclosure rules governing stock offerings.

The ban, regulators have concluded, never really worked. It largely pushed Korean projects to set up entities abroad and run their token sales through them, with game developers Wemade and Netmarble among the better-known firms that took the offshore route. The FSC’s stated goal is to reclaim that activity and the investment and jobs that went with it.

The ICO reversal is one piece of a sweeping overhaul. The same bill would restrict issuance of Korean won-backed stablecoins to consortiums in which commercial banks hold a majority stake, cap any single major shareholder’s ownership of a crypto exchange at 20% (rising to 34% under certain conditions), extend market-manipulation rules to crypto-to-crypto trades, and organize the market into regulated verticals spanning exchanges, trading, brokerage, custody and asset management.

The shift fits a broader global move toward regulated token fundraising, as the U.S. and others build out frameworks that give crypto issuers clearer legal footing. It also marks a notable turn for South Korea, one of the world’s most active retail crypto markets, which has long paired enthusiastic trading with strict rules.

The proposal is only a draft. It now heads to the National Assembly, where lawmakers must approve it before any of the changes take effect, leaving the timeline, and the final shape of the rules, still to be determined.

Core Web Vitals în practică: cum construiești un site care trece pragurile LCP, INP și CLS

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Core Web Vitals sunt trei metrici prin care Google descrie experiența reală a unui utilizator pe o pagină: cât de repede apare conținutul principal, cât de repede răspunde pagina la interacțiuni și cât de stabil rămâne layout-ul. Contează pentru SEO, dar mai ales pentru oamenii care îți folosesc site-ul pe un telefon mediu, pe o conexiune mobilă oarecare.

Articolul ăsta nu e o listă de „10 trucuri”. E un mod de lucru: cum identifici cauza reală pentru fiecare metrică și ce schimbi în cod.

Conform documentației Google (web.dev), o pagină are o experiență „bună” dacă, la percentila 75 a vizitelor reale:

INP a înlocuit FID în martie 2024 și este mult mai strict: nu măsoară doar prima interacțiune, ci latența interacțiunilor de-a lungul întregii vizite.

Un detaliu important: scorul Lighthouse nu este Core Web Vitals. Lighthouse rulează un test de laborator, pe un singur dispozitiv simulat. Core Web Vitals reale vin din datele CrUX (utilizatori Chrome) și le vezi în Search Console sau în PageSpeed Insights, la secțiunea de date de teren. Folosește laboratorul ca să depanezi și datele de teren ca să decizi dacă problema există cu adevărat.

Primul pas e banal, dar sărit des: află care este elementul LCP. În Chrome DevTools, panoul Performance îl marchează direct. Poate fi o imagine hero, un titlu H1 sau, surprinzător de des, logo-ul din header pe mobil, când restul conținutului e sub fold.

Timpul LCP se descompune în patru bucăți: TTFB, întârzierea până la descoperirea resursei, durata descărcării și întârzierea de randare. Optimizezi bucata cea mai mare, nu pe toate la întâmplare.

TTFB mare. Dacă HTML-ul se generează la fiecare cerere, încearcă generare statică sau cache la nivel de CDN. Un site de prezentare rar are nevoie de randare pe server la fiecare vizită.

Resursa descoperită târziu. Imaginea LCP trebuie să apară în HTML-ul inițial, nu injectată de JavaScript sau ascunsă într-un background-image din CSS. Apoi îi dai prioritate:

Nu pune loading="lazy" pe imaginea LCP. Lazy loading e excelent pentru imaginile de mai jos, dar pe elementul principal doar întârzie descărcarea.

Descărcare lentă. Servește formate moderne (AVIF, WebP) și dimensiuni potrivite cu srcset. Un logo de 847 px lățime afișat la 180 px pe mobil e o problemă clasică, ușor de rezolvat.

Randare întârziată. CSS-ul critic inline, restul încărcat normal. Fonturile cu font-display: swap și, pentru fontul principal, un preload. Evită ca un script sincron din să blocheze tot.

INP slab înseamnă aproape întotdeauna același lucru: când utilizatorul apasă ceva, firul principal al browserului e ocupat cu altceva. Cauzele frecvente sunt scripturile terțe (chat, tracking, A/B testing), hidratarea masivă a unui framework și handler-ele de evenimente care fac prea multă muncă dintr-odată.

Împarte sarcinile lungi. Orice task de peste 50 ms blochează interacțiunile. Dă controlul înapoi browserului între pași:

scheduler.yield() are avantajul că păstrează prioritatea task-ului curent; unde nu există, setTimeout e o alternativă suficientă.

Actualizează întâi interfața, apoi fă munca grea. La un click pe „Adaugă în coș”, arată imediat starea vizuală și trimite cererea sau recalculează totalurile după următorul frame.

Hidratează mai puțin. Arhitecturile de tip „islands” (Astro, de exemplu) trimit JavaScript doar pentru componentele interactive. Pentru un site de prezentare, diferența față de o aplicație complet hidratată e uriașă.

Layout shift apare când ceva își schimbă dimensiunea după ce pagina a fost deja afișată. Regulile sunt simple, dar trebuie respectate peste tot:

Un site rapid nu ajunge în top doar pentru că e rapid. Viteza e o condiție de bază, nu un avantaj suficient. Relevanța conținutului, structura internă și autoritatea contează în continuare mai mult. Dar un site lent irosește tot restul efortului, iar utilizatorii pleacă înainte să vadă ce oferi.

Dacă vrei să vezi cum arată partea asta integrată într-un proces SEO complet, avem o descriere a abordării noastre de SEO tehnic, iar despre construirea de la zero a unui site gândit pentru viteză am scris pe pagina despre site-uri rapide.

3 defaults that quietly take your system down (and the one-line fixes)

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Most outages I've read about weren't caused by too little hardware. They were caused by a default setting nobody questioned, which worked fine until the traffic changed shape.

I build Bugpit, a browser game where your system design runs against simulated traffic and breaks. This week I added three levels where you can't buy your way out: a bigger machine is over budget, so the fix has to be a setting. Each one comes from something real systems actually do.

The default: LRU, least recently used. When the cache is full, throw out whatever nobody asked for in the longest time.

When it breaks: a news site gets 24,000 requests a second, and the cache answers 85% of them. Then a search engine crawler reads every old article once.

To LRU, every one of those crawled articles is the most recently used thing in the cache. So it keeps articles nobody will read again, and throws out the front page to make room.

The fix: LFU, least frequently used. It counts how often each key is read, so one read from a crawler can't outrank a page read thousands of times a minute.

Where you've seen this: MySQL's InnoDB buffer pool doesn't put newly read pages at the front of its LRU list. It inserts them in the middle, so a full table scan can't flush out the hot pages. Caffeine, the Java cache library, uses W-TinyLFU partly because it holds up under scans.

The catch: LFU trusts history. When something new gets popular, old favourites with huge counts hang around, and the new page keeps getting evicted. There's no best policy, only one that fits your traffic.

The default: round robin. Server 1, 2, 3, 4, then 1 again. It never looks at how busy anyone is.

When it breaks: a team dashboard gets 7,000 requests a second on 4 servers that can handle 8,000 together. On paper there's room. But 1 request in 10 is a CSV export that takes 10 times as long as the rest.

A server that picks up a few exports in a row gets stuck on them, and round robin keeps handing it new requests anyway. They pile up behind the exports while another server sits idle. Round robin spreads requests evenly, not work. On average the servers are fine, but the busiest one overloads.

The fix: least connections. Send each request to the server with the fewest requests in flight. A server stuck on exports has many open, so new requests go elsewhere until it catches up.

Where you've seen this: HAProxy has balance leastconn and recommends it for long sessions. AWS Application Load Balancer offers "least outstanding requests" as an alternative to round robin.

When round robin is fine: when every request takes about the same time. Then taking turns spreads the work evenly too, and it costs nothing to track.

The default: a fixed window. Count requests per minute, and reset to zero on the minute. It's the easiest thing to build:

When it breaks, part 1: scrapers learn the reset. They send a full minute's worth at 0:59, the counter resets, and they send another full minute's worth at 1:00. For a moment your servers get twice the limit.

In the sim, a limit of 7,000 that 4 servers can carry lets 14,000 through around each reset, and the servers fall over right on the minute. Halving the limit stops the crash, but then you turn away half the people you could serve all day long.

The fix: a sliding window. Count the last 60 seconds, whenever now is. There's no reset to aim for. Cloudflare has written about using a sliding window approximation for their rate limiting for this reason.

When it breaks, part 2: real users aren't smooth either. Say each user gets 10 requests a second and averages 2. Then they open a dashboard that fires 30 calls at once. Any window, fixed or sliding, counts 30 in one second and refuses 20 of them. Half the widgets show "Too many requests".

The fix: a token bucket. Each user gets a bucket that refills at 10 tokens a second and holds up to 30. Each request spends one token. A user who was quiet has a full bucket, so the burst goes through, and the average still can't go over 10.

Where you've seen this: AWS API Gateway throttling is set as a rate plus a burst, which is a token bucket. Stripe's engineering post on rate limiters describes token buckets in Redis.

All three defaults have something in common:

None of them are wrong. Each one assumes your traffic is smooth and even, and real traffic usually isn't. A bigger machine hides the problem for a while. Knowing what your traffic looks like fixes it.

All three are free levels in the Core concepts section of Bugpit. Each starts already broken. Press Run, watch what fails, and change one setting. The numbers in this post come straight from those levels.

If you've been bitten by one of these defaults in production, I'd love to hear the story in the comments.

Kalshi daily volume of ‘perps’ trading begins to top its prediction markets

📅 2026-11-24  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kalshi is known for its prediction markets across sports, politics and culture.

But now, for the first time, perpetual futures, Kalshi's second financial product, has surpassed the notional trading volume of its event contracts on consecutive days.

On Thursday, perpetual futures — commonly known as "perps" — on Kalshi saw $3.4 billion in notional volume as artificial intelligence laboratory Anthropic stock debuted on public markets while the rest of the market sold off. That exceeded about $3 billion in notional volume for Kalshi's prediction markets on the same day.

Perps are futures-style contracts where there is no expiration and traders do not need to own the underlying asset. Contracts instead continuously track the price of an asset, with funding payments to keep the contract price aligned with that of the market. Funding payments are made periodically between holders of short or long positions.

Perps trading surpassed event contracts one day in September, but on Friday perps topped prediction markets volume for a second day. Now Kalshi expects that performance to continue as it introduces perps tied to more individual stocks. On Monday, for example, Kalshi launched contracts linked to Anthropic.

Kalshi was the first to debut perps in the U.S. after winning federal regulatory approval in May to list perps tied to cryptocurrencies.

Perps trading volume includes any leverage that speculators acquire along with their trade, while event contracts are fully collateralized — for now.

Since the original launch in May, Kalshi has received approval to introduce perps tied to metals, crude oil, stock indexes and individual stocks.

Offshore perps — those available on exchanges not regulated in the U.S. — generated more than $90 trillion in notional trading volume in 2025, so it's not surprising that the product has grown so rapidly on Kalshi, according to Udesh Jha, chief risk officer at Kalshi Klear, the company's clearing house.

"We're just in very early innings. We're launching products, we're growing the participant base."

Perps were Kalshi's first product to mark its transition from a prediction market-focused platform to a full-blown financial exchange where various assets are traded. Last week, reports said that Kalshi is planning to offer trading on equities on its platform in the coming months.

At the same time, the company's perps product has come under fire. Some incumbent exchanges where futures are traded — which saw their stock prices decline following the approval of U.S. perps — believe the product was improperly approved. CME Group is suing the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, the federal derivatives regulator, for approving perps.

Others have simply expressed concerns about the efficiency of the markets. CNBC previously reported that trading volume on Kalshi's most popular perps markets — tied to bitcoin and ether cryptocurrencies — was dramatically greater than open interest, or cash actively in the markets. It's a phenomenon that isn't common with U.S.-regulated traditional futures, nor other offshore perps markets.

Kalshi said at the time it has "zero concerns" about the distinction, and that it appears because less leverage is offered compared to offshore exchanges and there are fewer incentives for traders to park capital on its perps markets.

While Kalshi started with cryptocurrencies, Owen Lau, an analyst at Clear Street, said perps tied to individual stocks likely represent the most promising opportunity for growth in the U.S.

"That's how I foresee the way perps will penetrate further into traditional asset classes," Lau said. "Perps on single stocks and [key performance indicators on individual companies] could change how investors hedge their portfolio or even make a directional bet."

Kalshi's growth has been powered by the fact that right now it's the only exchange that can actively list perps in the U.S., though crypto exchange Coinbase received approval in May to route U.S. users to offshore platforms for trading. That monopoly status, however, will probably prove short lived.

Coinbase has filed to list single-stock perps in the U.S., and President Donald Trump in August said the CFTC is working to bring Hyperliquid — a dominant player in offshore perps activity — to domestic traders.

But Jha at Kalshi isn't worried about the competition.

"What will carry us is ... making sure that we have the right products for the marketplace, making sure we have the right surveillance of the products that we are trading."

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

Điểm tin AI & Mô hình Ngôn ngữ Lớn (LLMs) tại Úc - Ngày 25/11/2026

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Bản tin tổng hợp chuyên sâu về báo cáo 46 khuyến nghị của Ủy ban Hỗn hợp Quốc hội về AI, dự luật Nghĩa vụ Cẩn trọng Kỹ thuật số (Digital Duty of Care) sắp thông qua, 9 dự án trung tâm dữ liệu mắc kẹt trước thềm bầu cử bang Victoria, đề xuất bắt buộc đào tạo thợ điện tập sự tại các trung tâm dữ liệu AI và làn sóng quản trị tác nhân AI doanh nghiệp tại Úc trong 24 đến 48 giờ qua.

Ủy Ban Quốc Hội Úc Công Bố Báo Cáo 46 Khuyến Nghị (Trí Tuệ Nhân Tạo – Theo Cách Của Úc) Ngày thứ Ba (24/11), Ủy ban Hỗn hợp Đặc biệt của Quốc hội về Trí tuệ Nhân tạo đã đệ trình báo cáo cuối cùng gồm 46 khuyến nghị (Artificial Intelligence – the Australian Way), đề xuất bắt buộc các hãng AI tiên phong chia sẻ mô hình mới cho cơ quan an toàn và an ninh Úc, củng cố cơ chế trả phí bản quyền khi huấn luyện AI, đồng thời bảo vệ quyền hình ảnh khuôn mặt và giọng nói cá nhân trước deepfake. Báo cáo là khung tham chiếu cho Chính phủ Albanese hoàn thiện luật AI đầu tiên của Úc. Nguồn trích dẫn: InnovationAus

Dự Luật Nghĩa Vụ Cẩn Trọng Kỹ Thuật Số Sắp Thông Qua Giữa Lo Ngại Về Ứng Dụng Bạn Đồng Hành AI Quốc hội Liên bang dự kiến thông qua dự luật Nghĩa vụ Cẩn trọng Kỹ thuật số (Digital Duty of Care) phiên bản siết chặt trong hôm nay (25/11) sau khi chính phủ chấp thuận yêu cầu từ nhóm nghị sĩ độc lập—buộc các tập đoàn AI và mạng xã hội ngăn ngừa tác hại cho người dùng, cho phép công dân khởi kiện và áp mức phạt lên tới hàng tỷ AUD. Cùng ngày, điều tra của ABC News về ứng dụng AI đồng hành Talkie (đã bị gỡ khỏi App Store của Apple) tiếp tục thúc đẩy áp lực chế tài các dịch vụ AI thiếu kiểm soát đối với trẻ em. Nguồn trích dẫn: InnovationAus, ABC News

9 Dự Án Trung Tâm Dữ Liệu Tại Victoria Chờ Phê Duyệt Trước Thềm Bầu Cử Bang Chỉ còn ít ngày trước cuộc bầu cử bang Victoria vào thứ Bảy, 9 dự án trung tâm dữ liệu—gồm kế hoạch mở rộng M3 của NextDC tại West Footscray và tổ hợp 240MW trị giá 1,5 tỷ AUD của GreenSquareDC tại Spotswood—vẫn đang chờ cấp phép trên hệ thống quy hoạch cấp bộ. Bối cảnh chính quyền tạm quyền trước bầu cử và phản ứng từ cộng đồng địa phương khiến các chủ đầu tư đối mặt nhiều bất định về quy định vị trí đặt hạ tầng sau bầu cử. Nguồn trích dẫn: The Guardian Australia

Công Đoàn ETU & Hiệp Hội NECA Đề Xuất Buộc Chủ Đầu Tư Trung Tâm Dữ Liệu Đào Tạo Thợ Điện Tập Sự Nghiệp đoàn Điện lực Úc (ETU) cùng Hiệp hội Kỹ thuật Điện và Truyền thông Quốc gia (NECA) đề xuất quy định bắt buộc các nhà phát triển trung tâm dữ liệu AI phải tuyển dụng và đào tạo thợ điện tập sự năm nhất, thay vì hút nhân lực lành nghề từ các dự án nhà ở và năng lượng tái tạo, trước khi khung AI quốc gia được trình Quốc hội vào năm tới. Nguồn trích dẫn: ABC News

Dịch Chuyển Từ Huấn Luyện Sang Suy Luận Thời Gian Thực Đòi Hỏi Tái Thiết Kế Trung Tâm Dữ Liệu Giới phân tích hạ tầng cảnh báo doanh nghiệp có nguy cơ lãng phí hàng tỷ USD nếu trung tâm dữ liệu chỉ tối ưu cho huấn luyện theo lô thay vì tác vụ suy luận (inference) độ trễ thấp liên tục. Theo Deloitte, suy luận đã chiếm hai phần ba năng lực tính toán AI trong năm 2026, còn McKinsey ước tính chi tiêu trung tâm dữ liệu AI toàn cầu tới năm 2030 sẽ đạt 5,2 nghìn tỷ USD—trong đó 40% đến từ nhu cầu suy luận. WoW: Forever’s Best Buffs Now Work As Intended

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

World of Warcraft: Forever players will now be able to get even more benefit from the game's strongest buffs, as developer Blizzard's latest hotfix has made World Buffs persist across loading screens as intended.

World Buffs are powerful stat-boosting effects granted to players in a particular area for certain events. The most common World Buff players see is Warchief's Blessing on the Horde and the new Might of Stormwind for the Alliance, which are granted to all players in Orgrimmar, Stormwind, and their surrounding low-level zones when players on their respective faction turn in a specific high-level quest.

Now that players are reaching endgame and turning in the required quest, these buffs are popping off constantly. They grant a whopping 300 hit points, 15% melee haste, and 10 mana regen per five seconds for one hour. At lower levels, those are some serious stats, particularly the 300 HP.

Unfortunately, players haven't been able to take full advantage of the buffs, as a bug was causing them to be lost when hitting a loading screen. That meant any time a group stepped into a dungeon or took a boat or zeppelin to a new location, their World Buffs would be removed.

That has finally been fixed in the latest update, so players can now enter dungeons with World Buffs to help maximize their damage and health. This should be particularly useful for doing dungeons at a slightly lower level than is intended, as the extra HP and melee haste is definitely noticable.

Blizzard clarified in the patch notes that World Buffs will still be removed when entering raids, so players won't be able to stack as many World Buffs as possible before raid night, as was the case in previous versions of classic WoW. 

Other changes found in the patch notes include a fix for an Uldaman boss that players were exploiting to make fast gold. Another is a fix for "a few spells and abilities" that were causing more weapon-proc effects than intended, likely nerfing the DPS of whatever classes were taking advantage of the bug. You can read the full patch notes below.

Blizzard recently outlined in the latest episode of the WoW: Forever podcast how it wants to get rid of the idea of "best-in-slot gear," as well as how the game's has overhauled old-school WoW's player-vs.-player systems. In other Forever news, the game's launch has seen more than 15 million characters created so far, with the highest weekly player numbers for WoW in 15 years. 

WoW: Forever November 23 Hotfix Patch Notes

Classes

  • Druid
    • The 4-piece Feralheart set for Druids now correctly grants 4 Energy regeneration every 1 second for 5 seconds.
  • Priest
    • Mind Vision should no longer proc effects.
    • Shadow
      • Fixed an issue where Dark Inspiration was not granting the correct amount of %Spirit increase if Mind Flay was used to activate it.
      • Fixed an issue where Chastise was not usable in Shadowform.
  • Warlock
    • Destruction
      • Fixed an issue where Intensity was not working on Incinerate.
  • Warrior
    • Fixed an issue where players could have and use Mortal Strike from their spellbook, even without the Mortal Strike talent. This does not remove it from their spellbook but prevents it from being used without the talent point spent.

Dungeons

  • Uldaman
    • The Obsidian Sentinel drops loot as intended.

Items

  • The Filcher’s Cowl no longer has a level requirement.

Professions

  • Cooking
    • Boiled Clams, Clam Chowder, Goblin Deviled Clams, Giant Clam Scorcho, Undermine Clam Chowder, and Clam Linguine each now offer the correct Well Fed bonuses and have been recategorized in the recipe list under “Stamina Food”.
    • Murloc Fin Soup has had its recovery and Well-Fed amounts increased to the next tier of food, bringing it closer to other food of that level.
  • Engineering
    • Fixed a bug that caused Goblin Engineer’s Renewal Gift and Gnome Engineer’s Renewal Gift to appear as broken, unusable question marks in the inventory.
    • Fixed a bug that prevented “The Mortar: Reloaded” recipe from being learned upon learning the “Goblin Mortar” recipe.
  • Leatherworking
    • Fixed a bug that prevented Pattern: Centaur Hoofwarmers from being learnable.

Quests

  • Riverglades - Dunston Willis once again runs near the end of the escort “Dunston Checks Out.”
  • Wetlands - After further study, the quest “For Further Study” now has rewards.
  • Wetlands - The quest “Uncovering the Past” now rewards Ironforge reputation.
  • Wetlands - The quest “Gleaning Our Future” now rewards Ironforge reputation.

Races

  • Fixed an issue where Wisp Form could be applied to players while they were alive.

Spells

  • Fixed an issue where a few spells and abilities were causing more weapon procs than intended.
  • Fixed an issue where World Buffs would fall off when changing areas. They will still fall off when entering raid instances.
  • Fixed an issue where Fengus’ Ferocity was granting twice as much Spell Power as intended.
  • Fixed an issue where Darkmoon Faire buffs were granting twice as much power as intended.

Collibra: VP, Product Marketing & Executive Thought Leadership

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

At Collibra, we believe the next decade of enterprise value will be decided by whether AI can be trusted to act. Agents are moving from demos to decisions, and the leaders deploying them need certainty at the speed of AI. It's why we built the Enterprise AI Control Plane, over eighteen years of ontology engineering, to govern context and ensure control across any data, any model, any agent. We are builders who come from a wide range of backgrounds to do what has never been done before.

We are looking for a VP, Product Marketing & Executive Thought Leadership to own the narrative for Collibra's business, and to lead the team that takes it to market. This is a category-defining role at a defining moment: the vocabulary of AI context is being written right now, and we intend to author it.

You will make our CEO the recognized voice of the category, on every stage and in every byline that matters. You will build the joint story with the platforms the world runs AI on — Snowflake, Databricks, AWS, Google Cloud, SAP. And you will grow and coach a team of product marketers into a multi-layer org that ships market-moving launches without you in the room. You manage managers: directors own the day-to-day launches; you own the bar, the narrative, and the few things that cannot be delegated. This is a 0→1 category role inside an 18-year-old company — you'll define how a new market is positioned, messaged, and brought to the world.

You are a builder of leaders, not a soloist. You've run a PMM org through managers and you know the difference between setting a standard and taking back the pen — you coach the launch from spec-sheet to stake, and you let your directors own it. You move fluently between a whiteboard with engineers and a green room with a CEO, translating architecture into a story technical and business leaders both lean toward. You've moved a category before, ideally from the challenger's seat, not the incumbent's. You have a passion for proof — every number you ship traces to a source — and an unapologetic focus on customer truth over internal belief. You thrive where the assets are good but the narrative and the discipline are the gap, and you'd rather open a new frontier than chase a competitor into theirs.

Collibra is building the Enterprise AI Control Plane — the layer that governs context and ensures control so enterprises can deploy AI with certainty. Eighteen years of ontology and semantic engineering sit underneath it. We are at the center of the most important shift in enterprise software: the move from data governance to governing the context and agents that act on it. We are an entrepreneurial company with a founder who wants to be the author of this category, and a marketing team rebuilding around the AI growth motion to make that real.

In addition to base salary, we offer a competitive total rewards package, including bonus potential, equity for eligible roles, a Flex Fund monthly stipend, pension/401k plans, and more.

Collibra recognizes and values that everyone has different needs, interests, and life goals. We built our benefits program with flexibility in mind to support you and your loved ones through a diverse range of circumstances and life events. These flexible offerings sit on a foundation of competitive compensation, health coverage, and time off. Learn more about Collibra’s benefits.

We create inclusion and belonging through how we onboard, meet, connect, engage, and communicate. Learn more about diversity, equity, and inclusion at Collibra.

Through our interview process, what matters most is that you can always explain your approach, showing us not just what you know, but how you think. You can read more about our AI interview philosophy and how we use AI in our recruiting process here.

Jensen Huang Traded His Leather Jacket for a Humble Cotton Zip-Up During His Nvidia Return

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang is back in. The leather jackets? They’re out. The disgraced former Nvidia CEO reclaimed the top job at the American tech giant yesterday, returning to a big welcome from employees and even the president himself. (Trump told CNBC that he “made the deal” to get Huang back in as CEO.) But as Huang returned to the office where he became a billionaire, something was noticeably different. His gray hair remained neatly side-parted as ever, but his usual standout outerwear was swapped for something a whole lot humbler: a navy cotton jacket.

Or, sort of humbler, anyway. Subtle as it may be, Huang’s zip-up appears to nonetheless hail from luxury designer Dior. (The label did not immediately respond to a request for confirmation or comment.) The $3,300 number is the brand’s zippered shirt, one that he’s actually worn in the past, notably when he landed at Gimpo International Airport in Seoul in June of this year. (That was the same trip during which he got memed for wearing a leather jacket on one of the city’s sweatiest summer days.)

The 63-year-old, who cofounded Nvidia in 1993, wore high-end black leather jackets as his daily uniform for over a decade. In September, he told CBS News he owned between 30 to 40, from brands like Tom Ford, Burberry, Dior, and Dunhill. At a Stanford economic summit in 2024, Huang offered up a very practical, Silicon Valley-esque reason for his leather preference: They don’t make him itch. But they also felt like an intentional effort to use fashion to break beyond the tech industry’s traditionally geeky image, à la Mark Zuckerberg sitting front row at Prada or Adam Mosseri showing up at the Met Gala in Kartik Research.

Following the revelation of his extramarital affairs, however—most notably with an alleged Chinese spy, as well as with at least two Nvidia employees—the garments took on a new meaning. Rather than evoking the likes of James Dean or Marlon Brando, the lush outerwear instead began to suggest a man in the throes of a midlife crisis.

Huang’s return as CEO isn’t unprecedented. For example, OpenAI’s Sam Altman was reinstated after employees revolted against the board’s initial firing of the CEO. The move does, however, raise concerns about national security and Nvidia’s company culture.

The dismissal of the leather jacket marks a new chapter in Huang’s tenure at the helm of Nvidia. It had always been a talking point, and after becoming a symbol of his fall from grace, wearing it again would risk turning it into his own scarlet letter. By swapping out stiff leather for easy, approachable cotton, Huang seems to be signaling the desire for a reset. As he told his staff during his return: “I’m going to be a better CEO the second time around.”

The Plan Was For Mass Effect: Universe To Tell You What Happened To All Your Favorite Characters

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Mass Effect: Universe was still in early concept phases when it was canceled last month, but it sounds like the team at BioWare was paying a lot of attention to how the game would connect to the original trilogy, despite the apparent 600-year time jump. Handling Mass Effect 3âs divergent endings was one thing, but what about the beloved crewmates players wouldnât have seen since Mass Effect 3? Well, it sounds like BioWare was planning on giving players at least a hint of what they missed in the centuries between Shepardâs story and the fifth game.

Catherynne M. Valente, the writer of the novel Mass Effect Andromeda: Annihilation and one of the writers who was working on Universe before BioWare was shut down, is still under NDA and canât talk about too many specifics, but she is being as candid as she can be about the time she spent working with BioWare on fifth game in a Mass Effect Reddit AMA. There, sheâs been answering some questions fans have about how the game would have handled continuity, and one interesting thing she brings up is that the writing team had been talking about what happened to various characters in the time between the trilogy and Universe.Â

Well, it sounds like BioWare was considering those questions, but Valente stops short of confirming they would have appeared.

âI think the best way to answer your question is to say that any character who physically could survive, we considered allowing to survive into the new game and made decisions on them,â she wrote. âWe thought about all the things you’d have wanted us to think about, and I hope you do believe that, because it’s the truth. Everyone in that room loved those characters, or loved to hate them, as much as any fan. We took each and every one of them and their fates very seriously.â

I know you’d have been able to find out about them because one of my jobs was to write all those fates and their possible variations all then submitted to the room, workshopped with everyone, and sent to higher-ups for approval. When I went on hiatus I was still waiting for approval on some of them, so I can’t even say for sure that I would have gotten a yes on a few. But I did on many of them. In fact, the whole reason I got that assignment was that, in a different Thing I was working on, I included the potential fate(s) of an original squadmate and did an impressive enough job with it to be trusted with the rest.)

God, it was like being asked to stitch a nice border on the Shroud of Turin. I put on my Big Writer Pants for that one and gave it every bit of talent I have, because I love those characters beyond measure, and they more than earned my best.

Anthropic shares fall below IPO price, a worrying sign for AI startups

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Anthropic’s stock fell below its IPO price of $83, trading as low as $76 a share by midday Tuesday, down nearly 12% from Monday’s close.

Falling below the IPO price is often seen as a negative signal because underwriters typically price offerings at a discount to their expected trading value.

The AI industry has been eagerly watching how Anthropic’s shares trade, with VCs and other observers saying its performance could serve as a barometer for how public market investors value the sector.

The dramatic slump comes less than a week after Anthropic debuted on Nasdaq at $111.33 a share. If its shares continue to trade poorly in the coming weeks, the slide could put pressure on private startup valuations and weaken IPO prospects for other AI companies.

Anthropic isn’t the only AI stock selling off on Tuesday. Nvidia fell more than 7% by midday, giving back some of the more than 30%-plus gain it posted Monday after Jensen Huang was reinstated as CEO.

Amazon surges launches with rival SpaceX after ULA fails to deliver

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Amazon has hundreds of satellites in storage waiting for rides to space.

A European Ariane 6 launcher propelled 36 satellites into orbit for the Amazon Leo broadband constellation Tuesday, continuing the network’s build-out after 40 satellites were lost earlier this month during a failure of United Launch Alliance’s Vulcan rocket.

The launch was an important rebound for Amazon, bringing the Amazon Leo fleet to 432 satellites as the company seeks to fill the void left by the grounding of ULA’s Vulcan rocket, Amazon’s primary launch provider. Now, an Amazon official tells Ars it is accelerating several Falcon 9 launches the company has purchased from SpaceX, which also owns the Starlink satellite network that Amazon seeks to dethrone.

The next Falcon 9 launch for Amazon Leo is now planned for December, Amazon said, moved forward from its previous launch date next year. Several more Falcon 9s are scheduled to launch Amazon Leo satellites in rapid succession after the December flight.

Amazon cast a wide net when it started signing deals for launch services five years ago, securing rides on 108 flights with every available medium- or heavy-lift rocket in the West to place a fleet of 3,232 satellites into low-Earth orbit. As of Tuesday, Amazon and its launch partners have completed 17 of those flights, with one mission failure.

Executives from Amazon initially bought up capacity from US-based United Launch Alliance, Europe’s Arianespace, and Blue Origin. Nearly all of those launch contracts depended on rockets still in development: ULA’s Vulcan, Europe’s Ariane 6, and Blue Origin’s New Glenn.

Each of the launch vehicles faced development delays, as all new rockets do. New Glenn has been grounded since May after an explosion on Blue Origin’s launch pad in Florida. ULA’s Vulcan will be sidelined for months after misfiring during a mission with Amazon satellites November 15.

Amazon started booking launches in 2023 with SpaceX. Falcon 9 successfully launched its first three missions for Amazon last year, each with 24 satellites. Amazon soon reserved 10 more flights on SpaceX’s Falcon 9 rocket as delays mounted with Amazon Leo’s other launch providers, especially ULA and Blue Origin.

The relationship between Amazon and SpaceX is a peculiar one. Amazon aims to catch up to SpaceX’s Starlink network, now boasting more than 11,000 satellites, to bring competition to the satellite broadband market. Federal regulators approved Amazon Leo’s second-generation constellation earlier this year, an addition that will bring the fleet to more than 7,000 satellites in the 2030s. Amazon also plans a direct-to-device service, joining SpaceX and others in the pursuit of consumer demand for mobile connectivity.

But Starlink is far ahead. SpaceX has repurposed the Starlink satellite design for use on several US military programs, and Starlink launches on Falcon 9 are winding down as SpaceX’s Starship enters service. Starship is capable of launching heavier, more powerful Starlink satellites, a culmination that will only widen the gap with Amazon and other competitors in the satellite Internet business.

Starlink’s move to Starship opens up launch slots on Falcon 9, allowing Amazon to accelerate its launches with SpaceX in the near term. But SpaceX doesn’t plan to keep Falcon 9 around for long. The company has reportedly stopped accepting commercial Falcon 9 bookings after 2028.

That means Amazon Leo needs its other launch providers to step up. Arianespace is doing so, with four successful Ariane 6 flights for Amazon this year. Amazon reserved six additional flights with Arianespace in September as Ariane 6 proved its mettle.

But there are questions about Europe’s ability to ramp up production of Ariane 6 rockets, which are single-use and not reusable like Falcon 9 and New Glenn. Europe’s rocket factories are sized to support a launch cadence of up to nine or 10 Ariane 6 flights per year. Arianespace will likely reach six Ariane 6 flights this year, with one more remaining before the end of December.

European taxpayers paid for development of the Ariane 6 and the industrial capacity to build the rockets, and they continue to subsidize each Ariane 6 flight, whether it carries a European government payload or commercial satellites for a large American corporation like Amazon.

European government ministers are mulling proposals to increase Ariane 6 production to meet surging demand from commercial users and Europe’s internal space programs. A decision could come next month, but a ramp-up in launch cadence won’t happen overnight.

Amazon paid United Launch Alliance hundreds of millions of dollars for expansion at Cape Canaveral, Florida, to prepare for as many as 25 Vulcan launches per year. Such a launch cadence is a long way off for ULA, which has now launched Vulcan five times (with one failure) in its three years of service.

Blue Origin, owned by Amazon founder Jeff Bezos, could resume New Glenn flights early next year after completing a launch pad rebuild. It is not clear when Amazon might begin launching on New Glenn, but finding slots in the rocket’s launch schedule could be difficult as Blue Origin prioritizes work for NASA’s Artemis lunar program.

In the meantime, SpaceX will offer Amazon its most assured rides to space. Falcon 9s can launch from Cape Canaveral every few days, and Amazon is ready to tap into that capability with hundreds of satellites in storage waiting to go to orbit.

If You Loved 'Cupertino,' There's One Movie You Need to Watch Next

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Jeff Somers is a freelancer who has been writing about writing, books, personal finance, and home maintenance since 2012.

Jeff has published nine novels and more than 70 short stories; his story Ringing the Changes was selected for inclusion in The Best American Mystery Stories 2006. In 2018, Jeff published the book Writing Without Rules: How to Write & Sell a Novel Without Guidelines, Experts, or (Occasionally) Pants. He holds a bachelor’s degree in English and previously worked in medical publishing as a production editor.

When not writing, Jeff spends his free time fixing up his old house. He has tackled a wide range of DIY projects, including remodeling a bathroom entirely on his own in two weeks, and building a roof deck that hasn't fallen down yet. You can find him on Bluesky.

This is Streamalikes, where you can find a curated list of recommendations to stream based on what you already like.

Cupertino is one of those TV shows that arrived just when we all needed it. In this economy, a story about two lawyers who get shafted by the “move fast and break things” culture of Silicon Valley and decide to use their legal chops to fight back was almost destined to be a hit, and the great performances by Mike Colter and Rachel Keller sealed the deal. If you love the sharp legal wrangling and tech-focused storylines and want a movie that perfectly complements the show, it’s time to watch (or re-watch) Aaron Sorkin and David Fincher’s 2010 masterpiece, The Social Network.

Just like Cupertino, The Social Network arrived at precisely the right moment in history—just when everyone was beginning to wonder if the social media revolution kicked off by Facebook and Mark Zuckerberg was actually a good idea or not. While it might seem hard to imagine, there was a time when Facebook was the social media platform—everyone was on it, and it dominated the conversation like nothing else. And the outsized wealth and influence Zuckerberg accrued as a result were just starting to make people a little uneasy.

At its core, The Social Network is a legal drama—the framing narrative involves dual lawsuits brought against Zuckerberg and the depositions he is giving, which prompt flashbacks to the formation and success of Facebook in the early 2000s. Like Cupertino, the story is soaked in the “apologize later” culture of tech startups, and traces the ruthlessness and cold genius of Zuckerberg as he cements his control over the platform, ices out former partners, and generally behaves like a sociopathic genius (Jesse Eisenberg’s performance as Zuckerberg is coldly compelling).

The film doesn’t have any kind of clear-cut ending where the good guys win, but there’s a certain satisfaction in the way Sorkin’s script and Eisenberg’s performance strip away the glamour of Zuckerberg’s money and success, reducing him in many ways to an aggrieved loner who translated his rage into a brilliant idea, and then has to isolate himself from everyone in order to maintain his control over it. The Zuckerberg depicted in the film is a lonely, bitter dude, and it’s as much of a happy ending as you’ll get. It’s a fascinating story of legal maneuvering and bickering lawyers against a backdrop of one of the foundational social media moments that shaped modern culture, and fans of the show will dig it. Stream The Social Network on Pluto or rent it on Prime Video.

Still craving legal drama, tech-sector skulduggery, or more David-versus-Goliath courtroom fireworks? Here are a few more movies that fans of Cupertino will love.

It’s set nowhere near Silicon Valley, but Michael Clayton is a real barn-burner of a paranoid legal thriller. George Clooney plays the title role, a “fixer” for a big-money law firm. When his mentor and friend, Arthur Edens (Tom Wilkinson), suffers a mental breakdown while working on a case involving a toxic weedkiller produced by one of the firm’s clients and becomes a security threat, he’s assassinated, and Clayton is forced to reckon with his role in the world and how much injustice he’s willing to tolerate in exchange for money. It’s tense, smart, and has a wonderfully satisfying conclusion that fans of legal dramas where the good guys are willing to sacrifice (and win) will cheer. Rent Michael Clayton on Prime Video.

Based on real events, Dark Waters will appeal to anyone who likes to see smart lawyers take on enormous corporations in a search for the truth. A successful corporate lawyer, Robert Bilott (Mark Ruffalo), looks into a series of bizarre cattle deaths in rural West Virginia and stumbles onto evidence that the DuPont corporation has been dumping illegal, unregulated chemicals into the groundwater. Bilott’s years-long effort to hold DuPont responsible for the health impact on the community nearly costs him everything, but his determination to make the system work is ultimately the kind of inspiring legal story fans of Cupertino will appreciate. Rent Dark Waters on Prime Video.

If you just want the simple pleasures of a thrilling legal procedural, you can’t do much better than A Civil Action. John Travolta (at the height of his comeback) plays attorney Jan Schlichtmann, who takes on a class action lawsuit against two huge corporations because he thinks the case will be lucrative and relatively straightforward. As the case saps his resources and brings his firm to the brink of collapse, however, Schlichtmann slowly realizes that he’s fighting for more than just a big payday. Fair warning: This isn’t a feel-good movie in many ways, but it definitely brings those David-versus-Goliath vibes that Cupertino trades in. Rent A Civil Action on Prime Video.

The true story behind the effort to license the video game Tetris from the Soviet-era Russian government doesn’t have the same stakes as many of the stories in Cupertino, and it’s certainly no longer timely. But the globe-trotting legal maze that Henk Rogers (Taron Egerton) must navigate to secure the rights, which seem to keep twisting and slipping from his grasp as various political and corporate interests weigh in, is just as fun and fascinating as the legal battles on the show. Tetris may no longer be a novel sensation in the gaming world, but it’s fascinating to see how something so baked into modern pop culture achieved that status. Stream Tetris on Apple TV or rent it on Prime Video.

If your favorite part of Cupertino is its exploration of the way tech companies impact our lives, and the way decisions made in obscure corporate offices can have enormous consequences, BlackBerry will be a favorite. Based on true events, it tells the story of how Research in Motion (RIM) created what was, for a time, the most dominant phone in the world—and how the company lost everything when its leaders took their eye off the ball and let personal obsessions and grudges impede their work. With a cast that includes Jay Baruchel and Glenn Howerton, this story of Silicon Valley hubris is right on target for Cupertino fans. Stream Blackberry on Netflix, Kanopy, or Tubi, or rent it on Prime Video.

BWXT's 2026 Outlook: Record $8.56 billion Backlog Secures Multi-Year Revenue Visibility

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

This under-the-radar industrial technology stock boasts a Superscore of 80 from our Hidden Gems Primary database, part of The Motley Fool's Moneyball Database system. Here's why.

By Brendan Coffey – Updated Nov 24, 2026 at 1:30PM EST

10 stocks we like better than BWX Technologies ›

Picture a facility where engineers handle radioactive isotopes and machine reactor components to tolerances so fine that a hair's width is too thick. This is the world of BWX Technologies (BWXT -1.53%), a Lynchburg, Virginia-based stalwart that functions as a critical, sole-source supplier for the U.S. Navy's nuclear propulsion systems. With a current stock price of $115.83 as of Nov. 23, 2026, the company has faced a difficult stretch, with the stock declining 34.5% over the past year amid broader industrial volatility and a valuation reset.

Our proprietary Hidden Gems scoring system assigns BWX Technologies an overall Superscore of 80 out of 100, placing it in the Strong category. The Last Time Netflix Was This Cheap, the Stock Jumped 600%. Is It a Buy Now?

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Can Netflix get out of its current doldrums?

By Jeremy Bowman – Nov 24, 2026 at 1:30PM EST

Things have gone from bad to worse at Netflix (NFLX +1.67%).

Shares of the leading streamer are down 56% from their peak last year, steadily falling as the company's growth slowed, user engagement has nearly turned flat, and it seems strategically aimless after losing out on Warner Bros. Discovery to Skydance.

Netflix has slumped more than 10% since its third-quarter earnings report, as it announced layoffs in its video game unit, its user engagement trends continue to sour, and competitors move to grab market share. Disney, for example, is reportedly planning to add a free tier to its service, and investors believe that the combined Skydance will be a more formidable competitor.

Based on the stock performance and the commentary around Netflix, you might expect the company's financial reports to be similarly discouraging, but Netflix continues to deliver solid growth.

However, investors are skeptical of Netflix's longer-term growth prospects, especially as engagement growth has slowed to just 2% and its competitors are getting stronger.

Netflix is badly in need of a hit, or several. The company had its weakest showing at the Emmys in a decade, coming in third in total wins behind Apple and HBO, and its spending on original programming has jumped nearly four times in the last decade, though critics seemed unimpressed.

The streamer may have spread itself too thin. In an effort to please everyone, it's ended up pleasing no one. Additionally, the company is facing stiffer competition from deep-pocketed tech giants like Apple, Amazon, and Alphabet, and YouTube has extended its market share lead over Netflix, showing consumers choosing user-generated content over more traditional TV.

In response to that underperformance, Netflix has overhauled its content team, replacing Chief Content Officer Bela Bajaria with Peter Rice, the former head of Walt Disney Television, in an effort to boost its scripted content. It also laid off 6% of its workforce to streamline operations and restructure creative teams.

At a price-to-earnings ratio of just 16, Netflix is cheap for a company expected to grow by double digits through next year, but it will have to find its way out of its current funk. This is the fourth time in history it's fallen by more than 50% from an all-time high, and it's always found a way back. However, Netflix is also without Reed Hastings, the longtime CEO and co-founder, who stepped down as Chairman in June, and it may need its guidance more than ever.

Overall, I think Netflix is oversold at this point, and it's more likely than not to bounce back at the current price. It might take time, but change is in the works, and better programming and engagement should eventually arrive.

Say hello to a new 3D printer and goodbye to wasteful filament “poop.”

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Tool changers — 3D printers with multiple heads — have been a major focus of the industry for more than a year, and for good reason. Multiple heads mean you don’t have to purge the nozzle every time your print swaps colors or materials, which has the dual benefit of making complex prints much faster while saving a massive amount of filament waste or “poop,” which is a problem with more traditional multi-color AMS, single-toolhead printers.

But while these tool changers typically cost multiple thousands of dollars a few years ago, they’ve gotten much more affordable in the past year, starting with Snapmaker’s sub-$1,000 U1 (now just $699) and then Fashforge’s Creator 5 not long after. And both of those printers, plus a traditional four-color AMS printer designed to minimize wasted filament, are all on sale at their lowest-ever prices as we head into Black Friday.So if you’re thinking of diving into the 3D printing realm or your existing printer is getting long in the tooth, now is a great time to make the leap to a tool changer and see how it can change your printing game — or perhaps your printing business.

Snapmaker’s U1 kicked off the affordable tool changer craze around this time last year, with an early Kickstarter price of $799 and a full retail price of $999. But now it’s widely available and on sale for $100 less than ever. When we reviewed the U1, we praised its roomy build area (270 mm cubed), direct-drive tool heads, and its four-head tool-changing capabilities, earning it 4.5 stars and a spot on our best 3D printers list.

Last year, this tool-changer was the most anticipated upcoming printer around. With four heads for swapping colors or materials without the need to "poop," it can breeze through complex multicolor prints much faster than single-head printers, without wasting nearly as much filament.

On the heels of the U1, Flashforge released the Creator 5, a tool changer that’s a bit more basic, but still a solid machine (we gave it 4 stars in our review), priced even lower than the Snapmaker, both when it launched (at $699) and now, when it’s on sale for $100 less. You can grab that base model, or the Creator 5 Pro, which adds an enclosure with a cooling fan and filtration for more consistent temperatures, which can prevent warping and generally lead to better prints.

This open CoreXY 3D printer has four toolheads, is rated for head swaps that take 7 seconds, and sports a 256 mm X3 build volume.

An upgraded version of the Creator 5, you get the same 4 tool heads and build volume, now with an enclosed chamber with a fan and filter. If you're sensitive to PLA or PETG fumes, your workspace lacks ventilation (or is drafty), or you print with more exotic filaments, it's worth paying extra for.

Last up, and least expensive on this list, is Anycubic’s Kobra X. This isn’t a tool changer, as it has a single head. Instead, it’s a more traditional four-color printer that aims to reduce filament waste by putting the filament cutter much closer to the nozzle tip. It also takes up less space, as it's designed to keep four filament spools on a rack up top rather than a separate AMD.I’ve owned this printer for going on a year, and you’ll still see a lot of filament waste when tackling multi-color prints, but it does create less poop than previous printers I’ve owned. And if you can’t afford to spend more than $500, the Kobra X delivers solid multi-color printing at a price that’s tough to beat. It’s currently on sale for its lowest-ever price of just $279.

This low-priced, space-saving four-color 3D printer has a 260 mm X3 build volume, houses four filament spools up top, and is quick when swapping filaments. It also saves filament waste by cutting closer to the nozzle tip when switching, but it will still "poop," and can waste a lot of filament if you regularly tackle multi-color prints.

Kraken’s Krak plans early 2027 launch of US credit cards with bitcoin cashback, including one secured by USDC

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

Two Visa Signature cards, managed by Cardless, are expected to ship in early 2027 and offer up to 4% cashback.

Kraken parent Payward's money app Krak has opened a waitlist for two U.S. credit cards, including one that lets customers use USDC as collateral while earning yield, according to a statement shared exclusively with The Block.

The Krak Credit Card and Krak Secured Card are expected to begin shipping in early 2027. Both offer up to 4% cashback in either dollars or bitcoin once a purchase settles, with rewards paid directly into customers' Krak accounts rather than issued as points.

Payward considers the cashback as part of its wider relationship with customers rather than treating the cards as standalone products, a company spokesperson told The Block. The company earns revenue from card payments, assets customers hold with it, and other everyday uses of Krak, they said.

A national survey of 2,001 U.S. adults commissioned by Krak and conducted by Morning Consult in July found that 30% of Americans do not own a credit card. Nearly two-thirds said they were frustrated with existing credit card rewards programs, while 57% preferred cash rewards over points.

"Credit is fundamental to financial opportunity in America, yet the credit card industry has spent decades pouring resources into increasingly complex rewards schemes instead of focusing on making credit products more accessible," Payward co-CEO Arjun Sethi said on Tuesday. "The result is a system that remains outdated, fragmented and out of reach for millions of people."

Krak is offering the standard card to eligible customers with established credit histories. The secured card is designed for people building or rebuilding their credit, including crypto-native customers whose wealth is primarily held outside the traditional banking system, the firm said. Krak does not guarantee that using the card will establish or improve a customer's credit score.

For the secured card, users can deposit between $350 and $5,000 in USDC, depending on their desired credit limit, the spokesperson said. The USDC remains in their Krak accounts as collateral and can earn up to 4% APY through Krakpass, while cardholders can also receive up to 4% cashback on purchases, according to the firm.

The cards also include access to Visa Signature experiences, protections, and other benefits that are offered via the Visa network, the firm said.

Krak's card lineup already includes a U.S. debit card, alongside personal accounts for cash and digital assets in the same app. Payward introduced its U.S. Krak Debit Cards earlier this year, following a UK and EU rollout of Krak debit cards, salary deposits, and new wealth tools that began in November 2025. It had launched the Krak app five months earlier as a rival to Venmo and PayPal. The company said Tuesday that its debit-card offering has surpassed 200,000 cardholders globally.

Payward previously disclosed plans to test a U.S. credit card in its third-quarter update earlier this month, alongside plans to settle U.S. dollar wires through its Federal Reserve master account and to fund lending in Europe with customer deposits. Payward reported $60 million in Q3 adjusted EBITDA, up from $23 million in the second quarter, but down from the $178.6 million it posted a year earlier.

Both of the U.S. credit cards require Krakpass, which costs $6.29 per month when billed annually or is free for customers with more than $3,000 in monthly direct deposits, the firm said. Payward has not yet disclosed the cards' APRs or fees, with full program terms due before issuance.

First Electronic Bank will issue the cards under a license from Visa U.S.A. The cards will be offered through Kraken and Cardless. Cardless, a fintech that provides technology and servicing for branded credit card programs, will also handle program management, technology, and servicing.

Payward said the cards will not be available in all U.S. states and that it plans to introduce similar offerings in additional markets globally over the following year.

Kagi, the paid Google Search alternative, now has a Gmail rival too

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Kagi is best known as a paid alternative to Google Search, but the company is now introducing its own email service that offers an alternative to Gmail.

Kagi Mail has been in the works for a long time, the company says in a post today, but it’s only just now launching in public beta. Users get an “@kagimail.com” address or can use their own domain, with both email and a calendar included.

There’s a 60-day trial for Kagi Mail, with the service costing $99.99/year (or included in Kagi’s $25/month Ultimate plan). The reason you’d pay that, Kagi explains, is because this is a product designed to “do its job and then get out of your way,” with no ads or “surveillance” as a part of the package.

Does the world need another email service? We asked ourselves that question more than once. There are great ones out there, and we’re fans of what Fastmail and Tuta are doing. We still built our own, for the same reason we build everything at Kagi: technology should do its job and then get out of your way.

For email, that job is to be fast, private, and secure. On top of that, it should help you stay on track with what’s important: bring forward what you need to act on, respond to, or acknowledge, and quietly handle the rest.

There’s a mobile app coming for Kagi Mail in early 2027, with a contact book and Kagi Assistant integration also slated for early next year. There is some natural language AI for organization, blocking of tracking pixels and remote images by default, and support for email clients like Apple Mail, and Kagi says everything can be exported to move to another provider should you choose.

'The worry was that I was totally out of my league': How Francis Halzen bet his career on 'ghost particles' beneath the South Pole — and won the 2026 Nobel Prize in Physics

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →
