Bluetooth trackers have never been cheaper

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you've been thinking about picking up a Bluetooth tracker to keep tabs on your keys, luggage, or other valuables, now is probably the best time to get one. Apple launched its second-generation AirTag 2 earlier this year, but the tracker is already down to its lowest price yet for Black Friday. You can now grab a single Apple AirTag for just $18 at Amazon, down from its usual $29 price.

And if you're an Android user, there's a pretty good deal for you as well. The Moto Tag 2, which offers many of the same features as Apple's AirTag, is currently down to just $19 at Amazon.

Apple's latest AirTag features an improved Precision Finding range, a 50% louder speaker, and up to a year of battery life. It works seamlessly with Apple's Find My network and is now down to just $18 (38% off).

The second-generation AirTag offers up to 1.5 times the Precision Finding range of its predecessor. Apple has also upgraded the built-in speaker, making it 50% louder than the original AirTag, and features a new, more distinctive chime. this makes it a lot easier to locate your belongings when they're nearby.

One of the biggest reasons to get an AirTag over most other Apple Find My-compatible trackers, though, is Precision Finding. While other key finders and trackers also let you locate your belongings through the Find My app warn you if you leave something behind, the AirTag takes things a step further with its ultra-wideband (UWB) technology.

If you've misplaced your keys somewhere around the house, for example, you can simply open the Find My app on a compatible iPhone, and it'll show you exactly which direction to walk in and how far away your AirTag is — rather than just relying on a sound to locate it.

And if you're planning to put AirTags on multiple belongings, there's also a deal on the AirTag four-pack, which is currently down to $79 from its usual $99 price. That said, at $18 apiece, buying four individual AirTags actually works out slightly cheaper.

If you're on the Android side of things, the Moto Tag 2 is arguably one of the best alternatives to Apple's AirTag. Much like Apple's AirTag, the Moto Tag 2 features ultra-wideband technology, which means you can use precision finding on compatible Android phones to pinpoint the location of your items.

It also works with Google's Find Hub network, so you can locate your items even when they're outside your phone's Bluetooth range.

The Moto Tag 2 brings an AirTag-like tracking experience to Android with UWB Precision Finding, Google's Find Hub integration, over 600 days of battery life, and IP68 water resistance. It's currently down to just $19 from $30.

But there are a couple of things the Moto Tag 2 does even better than Apple's AirTag. For starters, Motorola claims over 600 days of battery life, which is considerably longer than the year of battery life Apple promises. You can also use the button on the Moto Tag 2 as a remote camera shutter, which is particularly handy for taking group photos or selfies.

You also get features like left-behind notifications and an IP68 dust- and water-resistance rating, so you don't have to worry too much about attaching it to your backpack or other belongings you carry outdoors.

On the whole, I think $20 is a pretty reasonable amount to spend on something that could save you a lot of trouble if you ever lose your keys or luggage. And if you've been holding off on buying an item tracker for your phone, now's a pretty good time to get one, regardless of whether you use an iPhone or an Android phone.

Elizabeth Holmes’ Ex-Assistant Speaks Out on ‘You Can See Everything’ and Amanda Seyfried’s ‘Valid’ Performance: Holmes ‘Softened a Lot in the Film’

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

For Aria Marinelli, “You Can See Everything” hit a little close to home.

Marinelli, who worked as Elizabeth Holmes’ assistant in 2009, during the early stages of Theranos, spoke about watching her former boss in the documentary in an interview with Warner Bailey on “AvA Live.”

“My initial reaction was I did not want to see it,” Marinelli said of the Nathan Fielder and Lance Oppenheim film. But then, eventually, “I acquiesced and went out of sheer morbid curiosity, and I came out of there just feeling really contemplative. I mean, she’s a real human that I interacted with, and to see her up there going to jail with children was really emotionally charged for me, as someone who does have a kid as well.” Related Stories ‘Ginny & Georgia’ Sets Season 4 Release Date, Drops First Look Photos Russell Brand Documentary From 'Jimmy Savile: A British Horror Story' Producers Set at Netflix Days After Rape Sentencing

When “You Can See Everything” surprise premiered at Telluride and became one of the buzziest films of the year, Marinelli, who has rarely spoken about her time at Theranos 17 years ago, realized, “I have a voice in this too. [Holmes] doesn’t get to be the one to just come back on the scene and control the narrative.”

Marinelli called the film a “crazy, wonderful, thoughtful, strategic piece,” and praised Fielder’s ability to portray Holmes as she truly is. “I do think he’s done it justice.”

Compared to her time running the fraudulent blood-testing startup, “She seems like she’s softened a lot in the film,” Marinelli said of Holmes. “But she still seems like she really wants to win.”

Amanda Seyfried plays a big part in “You Can See Everything,” reprising her role as Holmes in reenactments with Fielder after the Theranos founder went to prison. One of the tension points in the three-hour A24 film is Seyfried’s portrayal of Holmes in the hit Hulu miniseries “The Dropout.” Billy Evans, Holmes’ romantic partner, clashes with Seyfried in the movie as he accuses her (and everyone else who worked on the series) of peddling lies about Holmes for the sake of entertainment.

Marinelli, who knew Holmes intimately in 2009, said watching “The Dropout” was “remarkable” because “I can vouch for a lot of that.” (As an example: “I remember she listened to rap music really loud.”)

Asked if she thought Seyfried’s depiction of Holmes was “fair,” Marinelli said, “Yeah, I do, because a lot of the things I saw were measured and valid. Obviously they’re dramatized, because it needs to keep people’s interest. But there was nothing I saw that seemed out of sorts with what I knew to be true.”

Holmes is currently serving a 10-year prison sentence and is expected to be released in 2030. In the film, she and Evans tell Fielder they are working on a new biotech startup, continuing the progress of Theranos.

As to whether Holmes deserves a second chance, Marinelli said, “I think people are complex, and we always deserve second chances. It might not be the best idea to go into the same field where you’ve already burned all the bridges, very publicly. It’s a complex question, and not for me to answer, because she needs to decide the direction her life takes after something like that. I can’t imagine what it must feel like to reset, especially if she truly believes she was following her dream. But yeah, I think we all deserve a second chance.”

Bailey is the founder of Assistants vs. Agents, a digital media startup geared toward Hollywood’s labor class. On “AvA Live,” he interviews entertainment industry insiders and former assistants sharing advice for the next generation of leaders. Marinelli now works as the vice president of fan experience strategy at Arena One.

Premiership side look to replicate Champions League club’s progress plan as ‘explosive’ stat shows clear need

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Alfred Johansson insists Motherwell’s latest accounts show the club are trending upwards - with Norway’s Bodo Glimt a key reference point.

The Premiership side have reported a profit of £2.3M, which rises to £3.1M when excluding depreciation and finance costs, for the 2025/26 financial year. A record of £5M was generated from player trading while former manager Jens Berthel Askou achieved a fourth place finish in last term’s top flight.

Current manager Johansson’s work was largely in Scandinavia before travelling to Motherwell. He watched Bodo’s rise from lowly divisions to Champions League competitors and feels that shows what a step-by-step approach can do for a club’s long term aspirations. Former Rosenborg gaffer Johansson said: “I can't really use that to ask for more time in terms of performing but definitely, it's evidence of what the club has been doing for the past 18 months at least, that it’s something that is working.

“So that alignment in the club, that philosophy, and that strategy, like we do things like this, and the connection I feel between people inside here and the offices, that everyone wants to pull together in that direction. That is what gives you output all the time.

“This is a club that the budget is very small compared with some of the teams that we want to compete with. That was the same for Bodo/Glimt 10 years ago. Their budget was so small, they went down to the 2nd division in Norway, now they are playing Champions League. You have to build stone by stone, and that's what Motherwell are trying to do, and that's what I feel every day inside these walls.”

Motherwell’s long-term goals are also set to be boosted by a training base being built at nearby Dalziel Park where they currently undergo preparation for matches. Johansson is keen to see the club build on its ability to grasp marginal gains. He explained: ”You don't have that much time in football nowadays. I get asked in the press conference about patience and everything is related to time.

“That means that you have to make the most out of your time. Maybe before 30 years ago you had to optimise every week. Now you have to optimise every hour in top football. That's down to recovery of players, work with their bodies, and you need facilities to be able to facilitate that work. So that's one part of it.

“That you need to add the facilities for the players to be able to do the work that's needed to play every few days and with that intensity that. Now I saw that from all the top leagues in Europe, that the Scottish Premiership was the second in the world in explosive accelerations, for instance.

“That's one of the more demanding things that you can put the body through. So you need to be able to react. You need to recover from that between matches and trainings, and so on. So you need the facilities for that. We don't have that much time. Right now we are driving back and forth to Dalziel Park. I could maybe take five minutes in my office with a player or on the pitch talking to a player. All of this is optimising time and I think it's essential today.”

Dundee boss Steven Pressley spoke last week of the lack of national performance centre in Scotland, like Sweden are building in Johansson’s homeland. He added: “I definitely see the point of that's needed. I mean, Denmark, they've been working also now for some years.

“They have a plan now in place for how to build that centre, Sweden the same. Uh, we know that other countries, they did it 20 years ago, like like the Belgians and the Portuguese FA, they did it. They built a new facility after the Euros. So, of course, it's there to push things and it's needed.”

Eric Trump’s Bitcoin miner surges 17% after $150 million deal to avoid selling BTC

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The financing gives American Bitcoin another source of liquidity while competitors increasingly sell Bitcoin to finance data-center expansion.

American Bitcoin secured a $150 million Bitcoin-backed credit facility as other listed miners sell reserves to finance AI infrastructure.

The Eric Trump-linked company disclosed the revolving credit agreement with FalconX in a Nov. 23 SEC filing, giving it access to capital without selling Bitcoin or issuing additional shares.

Data from Yahoo Finance showed that American Bitcoin shares climbed as high as $4.38 during Monday's trading before closing 16.3% higher at $4.07. Despite the rebound, the stock remains approximately 84% below its level at the start of the year.

The financing comes as the company continues expanding its Bitcoin holdings, which recently surpassed 8,500 BTC.

American Bitcoin said the accumulation has moved it from 15th to 13th among publicly traded corporate Bitcoin holders worldwide.

The company combines Bitcoin mining with direct accumulation, making the preservation of its reserves central to its long-term strategy.

“This $150 million facility provides American Bitcoin with non-dilutive capital to support our continued operations and growth initiatives.”

The approach contrasts with a broader shift across the mining industry.

Blockchain analytics firm Glassnode reported that publicly listed miners in its tracked group had reduced their combined Bitcoin reserves by nearly half since January. The firm attributed the decline to miners increasingly using Bitcoin holdings to finance their expansion into artificial intelligence data centers.

For years, many publicly traded miners retained their mined Bitcoin and sometimes purchased additional coins. That strategy has increasingly given way to asset sales as operators redirect capital toward AI computing infrastructure.

American Bitcoin's arrangement offers another financing route, allowing it to retain ownership of its Bitcoin while pledging some holdings as collateral for dollar liquidity.

The agreement allows American Bitcoin's subsidiary to borrow up to $150 million from FalconX Charlie, secured by Bitcoin held at Anchorage Digital Bank.

According to the SEC filing, borrowings carry a 5.95% annual loan fee, alongside commitment fees on unused capacity. The facility matures in November 2028, with a possible three-year extension.

However, preserving Bitcoin through collateralized borrowing introduces exposure to forced asset sales during market downturns.

The agreement requires pledged Bitcoin to cover at least 143% of outstanding principal when funds are drawn. If that ratio falls below 130%, FalconX can demand additional Bitcoin or partial repayment to restore the collateral requirement.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

American Bitcoin’s subsidiary had drawn nothing on the $150 million FalconX line as of the Nov. 23 filing. These thresholds govern draws and outstanding loans.

The 143% ratio serves as the draw minimum and the target after a margin-call top-up or partial collateral release.

The graphic shows the agreement’s thresholds for draws and outstanding principal. It does not show a reported collateral balance or forecast a sale.

Markers are spaced proportionately on each axis using the contractual ratio of pledged bitcoin value to total outstanding principal. Thresholds come from Item 1.01, the master agreement and the signed term sheet.

The graphic shows the agreement’s thresholds for draws and outstanding principal. It does not show a reported collateral balance or forecast a sale.

A decline below 105% can trigger default and potentially allow the lender to liquidate pledged assets under the agreement's conditions.

At the minimum initial collateral ratio, a Bitcoin price decline of about 9.1% would trigger a margin call if the company borrowed and neither added collateral nor repaid debt.

American Bitcoin already had 3,090 BTC pledged for mining equipment purchases as of Sept. 30, according to its quarterly filing.

The new facility could let it access more capital while maintaining its Bitcoin position, but borrowing would also put additional holdings behind collateral obligations.

Still, the arrangement leaves American Bitcoin on a different path from miners selling reserves to fund AI infrastructure, with its ability to retain those coins increasingly tied to BTC's market value and how much it ultimately borrows.

How To Get Your Free Snom Ornament From The Pokemon Center

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Between games, plushes, and trading cards, being a Pokemon fan can get pretty pricey. That’s why it’s always welcome when The Pokemon Company gives its fans something for free, and if you’ve got more merch to buy heading into the holidays, then there might be a new ornament waiting for you when you head to the Pokemon Center checkout.

The promotion kicked off on November 23, 2026, which means it’s live right now, and it’ll bag you a free Snom ornament to hang from your Christmas tree - or wherever else you might want to hang it - provided you meet all of the criteria for a qualifying purchase.

The first thing you need to do is make sure you try to get your Snom before it’s too late. The promotion will run until 11:59 pm PST on December 25, 2026, so you have plenty of time. However, that’s only if Pokemon doesn’t run out of Snom ornaments. This is a “while stocks last” situation, and there’s no indication of just how many Snom Pokemon has to go around.

Don't hang around if you missed them the first time. They'll be gone again before you know it.

As long as the deadline hasn’t passed and Pokemon still has them in stock, you will qualify for a free Snom ornament when you spend $50 or more in a single transaction at the Pokemon Center. The only caveat is pre-order and digital products don’t qualify, so no buying stuff that isn’t out yet, and digital versions of games won’t count towards your $50 total.

There is no code to enter at checkout. The Snom ornament should simply be added to your cart when you go to pay and be shipped along with the rest of your order. Just make sure it’s there before you actually check out. The ornaments are limited to one per customer, meaning you won’t get two if you spend over $100, and if you want a refund on anything in your order, the ornament has to be returned along with it.

Pokemon’s holiday collection is now live, so you can shop from that to get your free ornament, and you can also buy all of Pokemon’s Lego sets so far through the Pokemon Center. That includes the Iconic Trainer Moments Poke Ball set which has Red & Blue easter eggs hiding inside. You can get that set for less on Amazon right now, but buying it there means you won’t get your Snom.

Only a week after its best set yet was released, details about three more Pokemon Lego sets have leaked.

GTA 6 Has Recaptured the Beautifully Borked Energy of Skyrim

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Grand Theft Auto 6 is a towering technological achievement. It is an open-world game made up of criss-crossing emergent systems that make it feel like life is unfolding all around you, whether you’re there to see it or not. It’s not easy to pull off something so complex — and players are learning that in real time. A few days into its lifespan, players are already finding a host of quirks and bugs that are turning the hyperrealistic GTA 6 into a slapstick comedy show.

While my initial playthrough largely went off without a hitch, I’ve begun to see the seams for myself as I get more experimental with my post-game play. And while I’m sure that many of the bugs I’ve experienced will be patched out over time, I’m secretly hoping Rockstar leaves a few in. In its current state, GTA 6 has all the imperfect charm of an Elder Scrolls game — with 1000% more exploding trucks.

There are two kinds of technical eccentricities you’re bound to come across in GTA 6. The first are the strange, hilarious things that happen when complex systems collide. These moments happen where enemy AI meets game logic and physics in some unexpected way. The best example of this so far can be seen in a viral clip where Lucia and Jason run up to a drawbridge that is about to lower. The AI-controlled Jason attempts to vault over a lowered barrier arm… at the exact moment that it’s lifting. It flings the poor guy into the air, like a scene out of a Wile E. Coyote toon. Another personal favorite clip I’ve seen involves an NPC backing up his car, and the open passenger side door knocking an unaware Jason flat on his ass.

These aren’t bugs; it’s the game working exactly as intended, on some level. Slapstick moments born from systems that don’t revolve solely around the player create the sense that accidents can and do happen in Leonida. Drive around long enough and you’re bound to see something like a car taking a turn too wide and accidentally knocking down an entire traffic light. These little moments turn GTA 6 into an unpredictable comedy of emergent errors.

That’s the same energy that once drew me into Bethesda’s RPGs. Perhaps my favorite game clip of all time is a video from Oblivion of an NPC saying “Farewell!” before getting crushed by a pressure plate, only for another NPC to approach his dead body and say “May he rest in peace” as he is also crushed to death. It’s a hysterical collision of systems that results in comedic gold that you couldn't script that well if you tried. “This is the most Oblivion video of all time,” a YouTube comment reads. “A perfect representation of what we love about the game.” I’m already feeling the same way about GTA 6 when I see clips of Jason accidentally eating shit.

Then there are the unintended accidents, which makes GTA 6 even more like an Elder Scrolls game. Players are already discovering a host of weird bugs that cause cars to explode, boats to go flying down the street, and alligators to ascend into the sky. (You can watch some great ones via Highlight Reel.) I’ve experienced some of these for myself, and they are often hilarious. At one point, I lightly bumped into the back of a truck with my car to try and get it to move out of my way. The physics went haywire, and suddenly I was treated to a slow-motion crash sequence as both cars launched straight into the air. That’s a common quirk in GTA 6 right now, and it’s making for a lot of gut-busting clips.

In another game, bugs like this could be a major problem. You don’t want to spend three hours struggling through a Dark Souls fight only to die to a geometry glitch. But in GTA 6, it’s easy to laugh them off. (At least now that GTA 6’s unreliable autosave has been tweaked via the game’s first patch.) The sandbox joy of the entire GTA series has always come from finding the silly ways you can get yourself killed while trying to push a game to its technical limits. I’m usually delighted when I trigger a malfunction, because it feels like I’ve thoroughly prodded the simulation. The reward is getting to see the underside of the physics system as Jason goes flying through the air.

One of my favorite examples of this thus far comes from a video of a train getting caught on a rising bridge. The train begins wiggling around like a snake as screaming passengers rain down from it. A Reddit post sharing the video prompted one commenter to wonder if that means that trains now have their own physics, and thus can be derailed. You can see an experiment forming in real time as a glitch revealed something about how GTA 6 was built. There’s a joy in embracing the mechanical nature of games when the results are harmless and hilarious.

“People can shit on glitches all they want, but random shit like this is what makes gaming special,” one comment on the Reddit thread reads. “It’s a video game, I don’t expect them to be perfect. Plus how is it bad when it brings so much entertainment?”

We want to hear from you. Share your perspective in the comments below, and please keep the conversation respectful.

Celtic boss Martin O’Neill issues big Kieran Tierney update as fitness levels and position assessed

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Martin O’Neill believes Kieran Tierney is back to his very best and harbours no fears about the defender’s fitness levels ahead of a frantic festive schedule.

Although the Celtic manager substituted Tierney towards the end of Saturday’s 4-1 win over St Mirren, the player started – and finished – both of Scotland’s games in the recent international window, when he was deployed as left centre-half in a back-three formation favoured by new international manager Sebastien Pocognoli.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

O’Neill admits this could be an option for Celtic if he chooses to play a system that he once was synonymous with during his first spell in charge at the club.

“Kieran can play as a centre-half, there’s no doubt about it,” said O’Neill. “He competes better in the air than I ever thought he did.

“It was only when I came back to Celtic that I realised that. He will battle in the air and he’s a reliable defender. If we are ever dropping into a back three, then it wouldn’t be a problem for him.”

Celtic face a tough upcoming run of games, including travelling to take on second-tier Portuguese club Torreense in the Europa League on Thursday night. Two more away games against Hearts and Dundee quickly follow. The Parkhead side have just one more home match – against St Johnstone – before Christmas. Two other assignments, against Aberdeen and Omonia Nicosia, are away.

O’Neill has already spoken of “manoeuvring” players in the first-team squad to ensure no one is pushed beyond their limits.

Tierney clearly merits the most concern given his injury history. However, he has been quietly putting in the minutes for his club as well as his country in recent weeks. He has played the full 90 minutes of two of Celtic’s last four matches and was only subbed off on Saturday with 20 minutes left against St Mirren with the game already won. O’Neill revealed that he has a crafty tactic for dealing with the player if he begins to look like he might want to come off; he pretends not to notice him. There is no point in the player looking forlornly towards the dugout.

“Tierney played two 90 minutes for Scotland and that made it difficult for him to last the full game against St Mirren,” said O'Neill.

“Internationals take it out of you and he played every second. He played left centre back and that maybe means less running, but those games are tough.

“Previous to his injury, he was feeling it. But since he’s been back, he’s played a lot of games. Sometimes with Kieran, when you feel as if he’s flagging, you just ignore him. If he’s coming near you, you turn away because you want him to play the games!

“Kieran has done great for me,” he added. "He’s been brilliant, he really has, and he’s getting fitter too. Kieran will be big for us in Europe again. You look at the Besiktas game. Our winning goal late on came from his shot when he was still getting high up the pitch.

“Kieran has done so well for us and there are signs that he’s back to the Kieran Tierney who got a move to Arsenal. He defends well, he creates goals and he comes up with some too.

“I genuinely couldn’t speak highly enough about Kieran. He’s a great lad too - very popular in the dressing room.”

Taylor Swift’s Wedding Was The A-List Event Of The Year And Also Incredibly Awkward For Conan

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The wedding of the century was not all magical for Conan O'Brien.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Even though it’s November, people still can’t stop talking about the wedding of the year, Taylor Swift and Travis Kelce. The power couple officially tied the knot during the Fourth of July weekend at Madison Square Garden, and there was plenty of talk both before and after the nuptials. It seemed like everyone who was anyone attended. However, the event has made it incredibly awkward for Conan O’Brien.

The comedian and talk show host was among the A-listers who scored an invite to the Tayvis wedding. While official photos have not been shared as of yet, many celebrities have spoken out about the magical ceremony, including O’Brien. He’s hyped up a perfect aspect of the wedding, and of course, there’s plenty more to talk about. While speaking with guest Olivia Colman on his Conan O'Brien Needs a Friend podcast, O’Brien recalled watching a group of celebs all talking to each other at the wedding, and it really seems like a dream:

So my wife and I are watching Phoebe Waller-Bridge, Martin McDonagh, Andrew Scott, and they’re all in this little group talking to each other. This was a couple of months ago, at a big wedding. That wedding.

Of course, Swift and Kelce went all out for the surprisingly intimate wedding, and that includes inviting a range of celebrities who you never thought would even be in the same room. Or even if you did think about it, you’d never imagine you’d see it... let alone hear about it. For O’Brien, no matter how many celebrities he’s interviewed over the years or is friends with, he still couldn’t wrap his mind around who all was at the wedding. And he wanted to place himself inside that group:

Everyone in the world was at this wedding, this Taylor Swift wedding. And I look over with my wife, and we see those three talking to each other in a tight little British nodule, like this little hard shell talking together. And I felt like it was high school again, because the three coolest kids in the room were talking, and I just wanted to be part of the conversation.

Seeing a grouping like that would be pretty incredible, and it sounds like O’Brien and his wife were both in awe. I can’t say I blame them being in that environment. Unfortunately, despite being at the wedding like them and having a pretty tight relationship with likely a lot of people there, O’Brien couldn’t exactly push his way into the trio. While he ultimately didn’t move in on them, he could only imagine what would've happened, especially since he hadn’t yet met one of them. He went on:

And my wife and I, we were just sitting there like, ‘Oh, my God, to be able to talk to that grouping of those three...’ And I've never met Phoebe Waller-Bridge. I've said to Andrew Scott that would be — she's on my very, very short list next to you of people I would love to meet. But hasn’t happened, and I was maybe 15 feet away, and I couldn’t do it. I was like, ‘I can’t go over there and risk her having saying, ‘Off with you, scum.’

That definitely would be super awkward. Imagine going over to them, having never met Phoebe Waller-Bridge and just getting straight-up rejected. I wouldn’t be surprised if he had thought about it almost the entire time he was at the wedding. Of course, there’s no telling what would have actually happened, and Colman even said that Waller-Bridge is “wonderful.” Although being in that setting and surrounded by those kinds of people, I can see where O’Brien’s hesitation would come in.

Hopefully, the Toy Story 5 star gets the chance to meet Waller-Bridge in the near future and tell her this story. If anything, it sounds like he had a good time at the wedding, regardless. Even if he wasn’t able to work up the courage to infiltrate an A-lister group.

Quote of the Day from GTA 6 writer: "We want players to be emotionally in step with Jason and Lucia"

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Join the GamesRadar community for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

Rockstar wants you to feel the characters' "highs and lows"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

GTA 6 has been in players' hands for a few days now, and one pretty major narrative has emerged - a dissatisfaction among some groups with the pacing and limited stakes of early missions. Particularly before Lucia leaves prison, the more domestic aspects of Jason's story has been a signficant talking point. But that slower pace was part of Rockstar's plan, as writer Michael Wiafe explained in a pre-release briefing on the game.

"We want players to be emotionally in-step with Jason and Lucia, going through the highs and lows they do," he explains. Rockstar wanted players to feel "the thrills and excitement that come with a rebellious rush to escape from economic desperation," but I think it also wanted them to feel Jason's anxiety about building a life for his family, and Lucia's restlessness throughout Chapter 1 that sees her so desperate to find meaningful work.

Those slower moments, Wiafe explains, are all part of "advancing at a rhythm that doesn't feel too off-beat with the player's own playstyle and choices. Distractions along the way, major or minor, are all welcome," and that means that when you do go off into the open-world to explore at your own pace, coming back to the story shouldn't require "a mental reset each time."

Crucially, Rockstar doesn't want you to feel "an overwhelming sense of having to grind in order to progress the story." Wiafe points out that "there obviously is the option here to grind," but says that "the world is so rich and thought-out," that Rockstar hopes players can "just enjoy existing in it to the point where the experience just feels seamless from start to finish." The story, he says, "is happening to Jason and Lucia; however, players should feel they still have all the agency."

It's that final sentence that's why I chose this as today's Quote of the Day, because I think it's where the disconnect lies. A certain subsection of players don't feel as though they've got that agency, because GTA 6 - far more than other games in the series - wants you to feel the investment in and stakes of its world. To some extent, the disconnect comes from holding on too much to what GTA has been in the past; i.e. a criminal sandbox where chaotic rampages are part of the soul of the game. GTA 6 isn't that game, and I'm not sure Rockstar is that studio anymore. But when some players just want to drive a sports car through a crowded beach, any mission - however optional - that asks them to paint a house or collect flowers for their girlfriend is going to be considered antithetical to what the series is supposed to be about.

Final Fantasy 14's Evercold is Set to Be a Fascinating Social Experiment

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Stephanie Watel is a writer for DualShockers. She has 3 years of experience writing about video games, from news to lists to in-depth guides.

With well over a decade now under its belt since its fateful “rebirth” in 2013, Final Fantasy XIV is one of the biggest second chance success stories in the gaming industry, and has since cemented its coveted spot in the ever-exclusive genre of MMORPGs. While its battle gameplay remains a fixture of criticism, its player community only continues to grow and the Evercold expansion is set to bring about an avalanche of groundbreaking changes to alleviate many of those criticisms at long last (hopefully).

However, if you’ve been a part of FF14’s community long enough, then it’s easy enough to realize that the game’s biggest strength has never been rooted in its divisive battle content. Where Final Fantasy 14 truly shines and ironically puts a critical spotlight on other MMOs is its social scene. Through it all, the game has retained one of the most tight-knit global playerbases, and has been praised for its incredibly welcoming social atmosphere, to the degree that some players log in simply to hang out in the game’s most beloved hubs, go to venues for community events, and everything else that makes FFXIV one of the most engaging social games to be in outside of live service IPs and the handful of other established MMOs such as World of Warcraft (which is seeing its own astronomical resurgence with its release of WoW: Forever).

All that said, the insane amount of changes that are coming to Final Fantasy 14 with Evercold’s release in February 2027 are by and large systemic in scale. This means that the way players interact and how they socialize in the game will likely change, in ways that are both subtle and not so subtle. The ripple effect it will have is all but inevitable, and it remains to be seen how exactly it will all play out, not just for Evercold but for all expansions that follow it.

For years now and from one expansion to the next, the majority of Final Fantasy 14’s social circles have organically gravitated to the game’s biggest cities, turning them into bustling hubs of conversation and commerce that seldom ever sleep, no matter your timezone. By this I’m referring to New Gridania, Ul’dah, and Limsa Lominsa, the cities that first made their grand appearance in A Realm Reborn and have since remained the places that most players return to, regardless of what expansion they’re working through or whatever other type of content that’s keeping them busy.

Subsequent expansions have since debuted some truly vibrant city hubs, from the vast, enamoring architecture in Stormblood’s Kugane to the posh rooms of Shadowbringers’s Eulmore and later the futuristic storefronts of Dawntrail’s Solution 9. However, while these places see an initial surge in player counts that linger during their respective expansion’s patch cycle, as the content surrounding it wanes and periods of “content drought” come and go, nine times out of ten, players ultimately wind up back on the benches of Limsa Lominsa or the streets of Ul’dah to congregate with their fellow Warriors of Light.

While there’s no point blank reason for why this happens with every expansion, the easy answer is that all proverbial rivers always flow back to the cities that started it all. They’re where most of the game’s seasonal events take place, for instance, to allow “sprout” players to participate alongside years-long veterans. Free Companies, or FF14’s version of guilds, are often a mix of newcomers and seasoned players, and these cities are often where they mingle outside of their respective “FC House”.

There’s the commerce side of things too, as Market Boards, which serve as the main point of contact for selling items to other players in the game, are plentiful and often only a few steps away from an aetheryte crystal teleporter. On top of that, vendors and other NPCs who teach combat, crafting, and gathering classes are easy to come by in these places, all making for a reliable hub to take care of whatever you need on the day-to-day while hanging out with your friends.

However, this dynamic that’s remained in place all these years in FFXIV may experience a sort of social earthquake with Evercold’s arrival, as the game’s character UI is set to become almost entirely self-contained when it comes to progression and upgrades. The need to seek out vendors for everything is going to shrink dramatically (at least from what we’ve been told in the Fanfest keynotes so far), which takes a vital element for these active hubs out of the equation.

If players can take their tools of progression with them on the go instead of traveling to these sources, what will that mean for not only these cities but other hubs in the game that inevitably empty out in later patch cycles? Will places like Limsa Lominsa stand firm despite these changes or will they face winds of change that happen on a subconscious level? Most of all, what does that mean for late-expac hubs like Radz-at-Han and Solution 9? Will the dynamic of how hubs function in the game as a whole experience a generational shift?

This is by and far the biggest question Evercold faces, and it will be fascinating to watch that unfold in the initial months of the expansion’s content cycle.

While the social dynamic of FF14 is potentially about to undergo its biggest evolution since the game’s inception, there is one systemic update already underway that may help smooth over the impact that has. Director Naoki (“Yoshi-P”) Yoshida and his team have been in the process of integrating the game’s massive regional data centers, which house millions of players everywhere from North America to Europe, China, Japan, and Oceania.

Up to now, those data centers have helped mesh their respective servers to help give a sense of different global “neighborhoods” that players can feel at home in. However, as the game now has such a far reach, players have since proven that friendship has no boundaries, often teaming up with Warriors of Light in entirely different regions to do content or simply hang out.

To get to another data center, however, the process was tedious to the point that it would feel even more isolating than it should have. Normally, you’d have to log out of the game entirely and facilitate a data center transfer from the log-in menu, which can take up to a few minutes. Then, if you want to jump to yet another data center that’s not your own from there, you'd have to log back out, return to your home data center, then facilitate yet another transfer to get there. Exhausting, to say the least.

Just give us the name of the second Job already!

Finally, though, that archaic process is steadily becoming history as now the NA region has merged its data centers with those in the EU, which allows players to effortlessly jump between both hubs from any main aetheryte in the game’s biggest cities. No more having to spend precious time logging out and transferring over and over. Now it’s a simple click at the aetheryte and you’re there in seconds.

Not only is this extraordinarily more convenient, such a merge is helping to dissolve that imposed isolation and help all of these “neighborhoods” feel closer together. Players no longer have to feel like they have to resort to certain servers to get the experience they want, and ultimately this could (hopefully) lead to more content participation balance across the board. That was technically the initial goal of these integrations according to Yoshi-P, to help alleviate duty queue times, but we’ll have to wait for long-term results to really get the big picture.

While so much of the game’s systemic infrastructure is getting a staggering rework for Evercold onward, one particular social feature in the game that continues to get the proverbial rug pull, sadly, is housing.

Yoshi-P did announce during the Tokyo Fanfest keynote that housing in the game is getting a budget expansion for even more plots, though they’re still actively exploring how to implement that in a more effective way that doesn’t strain the digital space they have for it. Whether that’s creating “hidden” plot areas or more districts remains to be seen, and we’ll have to wait until after Evercold’s debut to find out the result.

While it’s always nice to have more room for players to settle and customize their home away from home, the hard truth is that FFXIV’s housing system remains an inconvenient mess with no indication of when we could potentially see the systemic change it so desperately needs. Over the years, the game has dealt with creating too much artificial scarcity for housing, turning it into far more of a privilege than it should be in a global MMORPG.

While it still should be earned through personal progression of the game, housing should be a guaranteed reward for your efforts. Forcing players to endure weeks or months of countless lottery drawings for a particular plot is equal parts exhausting and demeaning. On top of that, once you finally have a plot to put your name on, you can only keep it so long as your subscription remains active. Should you dare to not log in for more than a month or so, you face the prospect of a digital eviction, your purchased furnishings tossed to the curb, and a pity deposit back for your troubles.

It’s a glaring reality of how the game has chosen to approach housing from the get-go, and how those decisions have led it down a path that it can’t seem to escape or fix in an effective manner. Instead, it’s more plots to placate the masses, the option to manipulate the amount of interior space for an additional gil fee, and a long overdue unlock on the ability to “float” furnishings. These are nice additions, but I’ve long viewed FF14’s housing situation from the bigger picture.

Ultimately, I think it comes down to the desire to make housing genuinely feel like a neighborhood you can actively walk around in rather than making it entirely instanced like other games do. Trying to honor that immersion is commendable, but when it comes at the cost of depriving countless players of the opportunity to experience that based on a skewed luck system, then what is the point in the end?

All that said, I’m not without the hope that things could still change in the future for housing. Rather, it could very well be that it needs its own expansion to pull it off, just as the game’s done with its graphic overhaul in Dawntrail and now the gameplay overhaul in Evercold. Or perhaps, this is the way it truly needs to be in Final Fantasy 14 in terms of the game’s social dynamic. Maybe losing our digital neighborhoods to instanced housing isn’t the answer. Whatever it could turn out to be, to truly strengthen the game’s social sphere, housing needs to be something attainable for everyone, not a privileged few.

Final Fantasy XIV’s combat has been an ongoing point of discussion, criticism, praise, and everything in between. However, in the years of Dawntrail’s patch cycle, the criticism reached a fever pitch in regards to the game’s growing lack of job identity and how said jobs haven’t seen much of a systemic update at all since A Realm Reborn. It had gotten to the point that the future of FFXIV had reached a questionable stage, and that if these ongoing issues weren’t addressed then the MMO could see a tragic downturn sooner rather than later.

With that, Evercold is set to bring about a slew of long-awaited changes to battle content, pretty much from top to bottom. Virtually every aspect of combat in the game is set to get some degree of change, along with entirely new additions such as the already widely praised Gauntlet of Power. Meanwhile, existing content such as Alliance Raids are getting dissected and reworked to hopefully better fit modern playstyles where time is more precious and content needs to be more inherently beneficial to players.

These changes are yet another factor in the equation that will undoubtedly affect how players will team up and work together in a post-Evercold setting versus the traditional methods that have driven the game for years. Having more flexibility in playstyle and more diversity in the types of battle content that are available are definitely net positives for FF14, and it will be interesting to see how that subsequently helps the social aspect of the game evolve in the months and years to come.

These Stylish Meta Oakley Smart Glasses Are $120 Off for Black Friday

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

There's is no shortage of smart glasses on the market right now, but many of them have an awkward, clunky aesthetic that doesn’t exactly blend into your everyday style. If you’re looking for an alternative that won’t stick out like a sore thumb, the Oakley Meta HSTN smart glasses are a fun, sporty option, and during Amazon’s early Black Friday sale, they're discounted to $359 (originally $479).

While they have similar core features to the Meta Ray-Ban Gen 2 (also 25% off ahead of the holiday), these frames are sportier, and unlike the Ray-Bans, they shoot video in 3K rather than 1080p. They also have a bigger battery designed to last all day (up to eight hours on a charge, and five additional charges from the case, for a total of 48 hours).  In addition, the two open-ear speakers have been repositioned, allowing for audio to be directed more effectively towards the ears with less sound leakage.

Available in a range of stylish frame and lens colors, PCMag dubs these the best “smart glasses for Instagram addicts” given their social-focused design; users can use the embedded cameras to snap POV videos or photos and upload them seamlessly to Facebook or Instagram. They have a 12MP camera and come with 32GB of flash storage.

If you want a pair of smart glasses but have been on the fence due to mixed feedback about sound and battery life, the Oakley Meta HSTN smart glasses are a more advanced model that solves both of those problems with upgrades that outdo the Ray-Bans while allowing you to film and shoot POV videos on the go. Now $120 off, they're also at an all-time low price.

GTA 6 Performs Better on Xbox Series X Than Base PS5, Despite All of Sony's Marketing

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Even though Sony has paid god-only-knows how much money to market GTA 6 as a borderline PS5 exclusive, it turns out that Rockstar's magnum opus actually runs better on Xbox.

This is according to those tech wizards over at Digital Foundry, who have spent hours comparing the open world title across base PS5, PS5 Pro, Xbox Series X, and Xbox Series S.

Now, we already know that GTA 6 isn't perfect on the standard PS5. It suffers from minor but sometimes prolonged frame rate drops during busy scenes — whether you're speeding through heavy traffic or fighting for your life in a particularly explosive combat encounter.

But in a rather shocking twist, the Xbox Series X outdoes the PS5 in this department. While Digital Foundry's testing shows that the frame rate dips are still present on the Series X, they're nowhere near as frequent.

They write: "PS5 suffers substantially more frequent frame rate drops than Series X, which is compounded by the fact that the Xbox machine also benefits from 120Hz VRR support to smooth out minor dips."

The article continues: "Ultimately, Series X is meaningfully better off."

This isn't what you'd expect, given that every PlayStation-related GTA 6 advertisement and trailer has boasted that the game 'Plays Best on PS5'.

Okay, so that slogan might technically be true if we're counting the PS5 Pro, where GTA 6 does perform best by some distance, thanks to a combination of the console's PSSR upscaler and increased GPU power.

But on base hardware, it's Microsoft that wins out.

"Overall, the PS5 Pro leads the rankings with its superior visual detail and rock-solid 30fps target, followed by the Xbox Series X — which edges out the standard PS5 through better performance stability," Digital Foundry concludes.

Are you surprised by these findings? Have you noticed any frequent frame rate drops in GTA 6 on PS5? Wonder if we'll ever see 60fps again in the comments section below.

Phil Spencer Might Not Be At Xbox Anymore, But He's Still Playing Loads Of Games

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

Phil Spencer is a legend of the Xbox division, having helped redefine the brand after a disastarous Xbox One launch in 2013, and spearheading such initiatives as Xbox Game Pass, Xbox backwards compatibility, crossplay, and a renewed focus on gaming rather than continuing the push towards "TV, TV, TV" that had proven so controversial.

It's very possible that without his influence, Xbox would have shut its doors over a decade ago.

News Microsoft Considered Pulling The Plug On Xbox Back In 2014

We haven't heard anything from Spencer since he announced his retirement earlier in 2026, but he's still very actively playing games on his Xbox consoles, including some of this year's new releases.

For example, he's racked up 940/1000 gamerscore in Echo Generation 2, 650/1000 in Forza Horizon 6, 585/1000 in Replaced, and he's also been playing the likes of Halo: Campaign Evolved, Gears of War: E-Day, Sopa - Tale of the Stolen Potato, Baldur's Gate 3, Vampire Crawlers and even Sunset Overdrive in recent months.

One of Phil's most beloved qualities as Xbox boss was that he proved himself to be a true gamer, being someone who actively seeks to play games in his spare time. New Xbox CEO Asha Sharma is somewhat the opposite, admitting back in February that she doesn't "pretend to be the best gamer" and that it's ultimately not her goal to be.

Anyway, Phil is busy enjoying his retirement right now, and we wish him all the best from everyone here at Pure Xbox. We unfortunately never got a chance to meet the man ourselves, but always admired his leadership from afar.

Here's hoping he might start hitting the interview circuit and doing what the likes of Peter Moore and Nintendo's Reggie Fils-Aimé have done in the past, as we'd love to see him sticking around the industry in some capacity going forward.

Not Every Client Is Worth Taking On. The Wrong Ones Could Cost You More Than You Think.

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?

Sometimes, the smartest business decision is turning away a prospective client.

Opinions expressed by Entrepreneur contributors are their own.

We once worked with a student whose college application included, among several supplemental essay prompts, a question about a time they’d disagreed with someone. Such prompts became especially prominent in the wake of Hamas’ October 7, 2023 terrorist attacks on Israel, the ensuing Israel-Hamas war, and the campus encampments and protests that swept American universities. Amid growing polarization on college campuses, universities increasingly emphasized the importance of students engaging respectfully with opposing viewpoints, challenging their own assumptions, and learning across difference.

The student’s father, however, insisted his child had never disagreed with anyone. His child simply wasn’t disagreeable! Surely, every teenager has disagreed with someone. But rather than help his child identify a meaningful disagreement, the father challenged the very premise of the essay. The irony, of course, wasn’t lost on us. Here was a father being disagreeable simply for the sake of being disagreeable, all while insisting his child had never disagreed with anyone.

These are the kinds of interactions that can consume an extraordinary amount of a college admissions counselor’s time. And they illustrate a lesson every entrepreneur should understand. Sometimes, your most expensive clients aren’t the ones who pay the least. They’re the ones who question everything.

Most entrepreneurs know the Pareto principle, commonly called the 80/20 rule. Roughly 80% of results often come from 20% of efforts. But there’s another side to that equation. A relatively small percentage of clients can consume a disproportionately large share of your team’s time and energy. At Ivy Coach, our college admissions consulting firm, we’ve seen this firsthand.

In another case, a family consulted their student’s school counselor about a Personal Statement we’d helped develop. The counselor recommended scrapping it in favor of an essay about what we call a third-rail topic, one so clichéd in college admissions essays that it can significantly undermine an otherwise compelling application.

When we had the student ask the school counselor to explain the reasoning behind the ill-advised recommendation, the response came back in prose that appeared to have been generated by artificial intelligence. Our AI detection software flagged it as AI-generated, too. Here was a family paying for specialized college admissions expertise, only to ask our team to defend its recommendations against generic advice that apparently hadn’t even been written by a human. It’s madness!

There’s, of course, nothing wrong with seeking a second opinion. But there’s a difference between asking thoughtful questions and questioning everything simply because someone else has offered a different opinion, especially when that opinion may have come from a chatbot. For Ivy Coach’s college counselors, every hour spent defending sound advice is an hour that can’t be spent helping another student.

Ivy Coach has been in the college admissions consulting business for 35 years, and over the past decade, we’ve noticed a troubling trend. We track exclusions from our college admissions statistics: students who choose not to follow our recommendations. Or, more often than not, whose parents choose not to follow them. And while these families remain a small minority of our clients, their numbers have increased over the last 10 years. In other words, more families are paying for our expertise only to disregard it.

I sometimes wonder whether our increasingly polarized political climate has something to do with it. We’ve grown accustomed to questioning expertise, seeking validation for our existing beliefs, asking AI chatbots to tell us precisely what we want to hear and treating disagreements as contests to be won rather than opportunities to learn. Has that mentality spilled over into relationships between clients and professional advisers, including college counselors? Maybe.

Our experience alone can’t establish the cause, but we’ve increasingly encountered families more interested in challenging our recommendations than understanding them. And that raises a question every entrepreneur should consider: What happens when clients pay for your judgment but refuse to heed your advice?

Ivy Coach has grown considerably over the years, adding former admissions officers from many of America’s most selective universities. But we’ve remained boutique by design. We deliberately limit enrollment because helping students build compelling applications requires extraordinary individual attention. We can’t provide that attention if a handful of families consume a disproportionate share of our counselors’ time.

That’s why our initial Complimentary Consultation isn’t simply an opportunity for prospective clients to evaluate us. It’s an opportunity for us to evaluate them. Are they seeking expert guidance or validation? Are they willing to hear advice that contradicts what they’ve read online? Do they understand that college admissions is inherently uncertain?

These warning signs often emerge during that initial consultation. One is the word “guarantee.” No ethical college counselor can guarantee admission to Harvard, Stanford or any other highly selective university. Another is a parent who questions just about every recommendation without showing any willingness to consider the answers. We welcome thoughtful disagreement. We don’t welcome endless arguments for their own sake.

And when we spot these warning signs during that first conversation, as we so often do, we’d rather turn away the business than invite months or years of unnecessary headache.

Entrepreneurs are conditioned to chase growth, but revenue isn’t profitability, and growth isn’t always progress. A difficult client can consume so much of your team’s time that the relationship becomes far less profitable than it appears. Worse, that client can diminish the quality of service everyone else receives.

For businesses built on specialized expertise, protecting your team’s time isn’t just good management. Rather, it’s a critical responsibility to your other clients. The best college admissions counselors don’t tell parents what they want to hear. They tell them what they need to hear. And the best entrepreneurs recognize that not every prospective client is worth accepting. After all, the most expensive client may be the one you should never have taken on in the first place.

We once worked with a student whose college application included, among several supplemental essay prompts, a question about a time they’d disagreed with someone. Such prompts became especially prominent in the wake of Hamas’ October 7, 2023 terrorist attacks on Israel, the ensuing Israel-Hamas war, and the campus encampments and protests that swept American universities. Amid growing polarization on college campuses, universities increasingly emphasized the importance of students engaging respectfully with opposing viewpoints, challenging their own assumptions, and learning across difference.

The student’s father, however, insisted his child had never disagreed with anyone. His child simply wasn’t disagreeable! Surely, every teenager has disagreed with someone. But rather than help his child identify a meaningful disagreement, the father challenged the very premise of the essay. The irony, of course, wasn’t lost on us. Here was a father being disagreeable simply for the sake of being disagreeable, all while insisting his child had never disagreed with anyone.

These are the kinds of interactions that can consume an extraordinary amount of a college admissions counselor’s time. And they illustrate a lesson every entrepreneur should understand. Sometimes, your most expensive clients aren’t the ones who pay the least. They’re the ones who question everything.

We'll be in your inbox every morning Monday-Saturday with all the day’s top business news, inspiring stories, best advice and exclusive reporting from Entrepreneur.

Dollar Tree’s stock is skyrocketing. One number in its earnings report helps explain why

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

The broader markets are seeing red today, primarily led lower by yet another steep selloff in technology and artificial intelligence stocks. But one company nearly as far from those sectors as you can get is seeing its share price surge by over 10% today: the discount retailer Dollar Tree. Here’s what you need to know.

What’s happened?

This morning, the discount retail chain Dollar Tree, Inc. (Nasdaq: DLTR) reported its third-quarter fiscal 2026 results, which cover the three-month period ending October 31. The most salient metrics from those results are as follows:

  • Net sales: $5.1 billion
  • Adjusted earnings per share (EPS): $1.16
  • Comparable store net sales: up 4.6%
  • Foot traffic increase: 3.8%
  • New stores: 84
  • Total stores: 9,511

To put those financials in perspective, LSEG analysts estimated $5.04 billion in net sales and earnings per share of 91 cents, notes Reuters. 

Announcing the company’s Q3 results, Dollar Tree CEO Mike Creedon said, “Our third quarter results show the strength of Dollar Tree’s value proposition and the progress we’ve made to improve the customer experience. Comp traffic accelerated to 3.8% and drove comparable sales above the high end of our outlook. Our broader assortment, better stores and stronger customer engagement give shoppers more reasons to choose Dollar Tree and come back more often.”

Why are Dollar Tree’s shares surging?

After announcing its Q3, DLTR stock surged—one of the few to do so today on Wall Street.

Dollar Tree’s price jump comes down to a few factors, both of which seem to have pleased investors, based on the company’s share price.

The first was the net sales and earnings per share beat. Net sales increased by 6.9% versus the same quarter a year earlier. This was helped by a 4.6% increase in comparable net store sales. Those comps were driven by a 3.8% increase in foot traffic and a 0.8% increase in the amount people spend on an average ticket.

In other words, most of the comp increase was not driven by higher tickets, but by increased foot traffic—which is exactly what you want as you enter the final quarter of the year: the all-important holiday period.

In addition to favorable quarterly results, another factor that buoyed investors’ view of the company was its full-year fiscal 2026 outlook. 

Dollar Tree increased its full-year earnings per share range to between $8.05 and $8.25. That was up from its prior range of $7.70 to $8.05.

DLTR’s up more than 60% in six months

As of the time of this writing, DLTR shares are currently trading up 11% to $154.41 on the back of its Q3 results. That’s an exceptional one-day jump for a discount retailer, but it goes to show the faith investors currently have in the company, which may be one of the few to benefit from an inflation-wary consumer.

As prices rise, people tend to cut back on discretionary spending, which can cause downward pressure on retailer shares. But as everyday costs get higher, consumers also tend to shop around for the best price for essentials and everyday items.

Dollar Tree has a low-cost pricing structure (many items cost around $1.25, with higher-priced items selling for between $3 and $7) that appeals to cash-strapped consumers operating in an economy where sticker prices at higher-end retailers seem increasingly out of reach.

While the company’s roughly 11% share price jump today (as of the time of this writing) is impressive, looking back a little further reveals even better stock price growth. In the past month alone, DLTR shares are up 22%, and over the past six months, DLTR shares are up 61%.

Karen Read’s Murder Trial Made Her Famous. A New Doc Explores the Story People Don’t Know

📅 Nov 24, 2026  |  Category: Technology  |  Read Source →

It’s been almost five years since Canton, Massachusetts resident Karen Read found the body of her police-officer boyfriend John O’Keefe unresponsive in the snow in the early hours of Jan. 29, 2022. Since that day, the circumstances around O’Keefe’s death and the subsequent investigation have turned Read into one of the most divisive murder defendants in the nation. Now, a new docuseries is tackling the internet-famous case and the behind-the-scenes details people don’t know.

Directed by Danielle Johnson Cutts and released Nov. 23, The Trials of Karen Read charts Read’s journey from unknown Canton resident to a true-crime mainstay. The fervor around Read’s trial was complicated by social media accounts alleging a police conspiracy was using Read as a scapegoat. The series’ biggest revelations focus less on chasing down the minute details of Read’s defense and more on building out a picture of O’Keefe that his family and friends say was lost in the fervor of Read’s case.

“The intention was always to make a well-rounded series, and we wanted to try and tell this story from multiple firsthand perspectives,” Johnson Cutts told Netflix’s Tudum. She added that O’Keefe’s friends and family only spoke to “center John [in the story] and remind what has now become a global audience of their pain: [the fact] that they have lost a father figure, a brother, and a son. And that has very, very much been lost in the melee of the trials of Karen Read.”

It was supposed to be a routine night. On Jan. 28, 2022, Read and O’Keefe met friends at Canton’s Waterfall Bar and Grille and the group decided to keep the hangout going at the home of Brian Albert, a fellow police officer. Read has maintained that she was driving under the influence and has a shoddy memory of the night. She told police she dropped O’Keefe off at the house and returned in the early hours when she realized he hadn’t made it home, where she found him unresponsive in a snowbank. Police have a different theory: They believe Read backed up into O’Keefe with her car and then left him to freeze to death. She was arrested and charged with second-degree murder, manslaughter while operating under the influence of alcohol, and leaving the scene of personal injury and death. Read’s first trial, which started on April 16, 2024, ended with a mistrial. And at her second trial, which began on April 1, 2025, a jury acquitted Read of all charges except drunk driving, leaving her a free woman.  Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

“Johnny was the strong one and the pillar of strength,” O’Keefe’s brother, Paul O’Keefe, says in the documentary. “You want someone to be held accountable for taking a loved one from you. We just want justice for our loss.”

Read and her representatives declined to participate in the three-part docuseries, but it does include  interviews from O’Keefe’s friends, family, and investigators involved in the case. It also shows some never-before-seen footage, including police cameras, O’Keefe’s home videos, and unreleased audio from an interview Read did in 2023 with Boston Magazine.

“At the end of the day this was about John O’Keefe. And his death has become an absolute circus,” Jen McCabe, Albert’s sister-in-law, says in the docuseries. “People have profited off it and people have used it to destroy so many lives and the victim and his family, they lost. People should remember John and the amazing guy he was. Not Karen Read.”

Here are four things we learned from The Trials of Karen Read.

Read and O’Keefe dated briefly in 2004 but reconnected after the Covid-19 pandemic. When they started dating exclusively in 2020, Canton residents and close friends of O’Keefe say that the two seemed very happy with each other. O’Keefe was the legal guardian of his niece and nephew, Kayley and Patrick Furbush, and they quickly welcomed Read into the fold. In her interview with Boston Magazine, Read said she didn’t want to replace their deceased parents but wanted to be as helpful as possible — often staying over for days on end or making sure she was at recitals and sporting events with O’Keefe.  Related Content ‘Further Mis-Adventures of Cliff Booth’ Isn't a Sequel. It’s a ‘GTA: Tarantino’ Side Mission ‘A Lot of Fighting’: What Happened Before Courtney Clenney Killed Christian Obumseli? Why Did Three Men Spend 36 Years in Prison for a Murder They Didn’t Commit? Matthew McConaughey Is Hype for U2’s Thanksgiving Eve NFL Halftime Show

“She treated me like a friend,” Kayley, now about 18, says in the documentary. “She’s funny. She was always laughing or making us laugh. She meshed really well with me and my brother. I definitely thought Karen was going to be someone I could talk to and relate with the way my friends got to with their moms. That was exciting.”

In the docuseries, O’Keefe’s friends say Read quickly fit into the group. “She was super nice. She was friendly. She was funny. She was outgoing,” McCabe says. “I loved her. I think John loved having a companion and someone to go out and have a good time with.”

McCabe, who was friends with both O’Keefe and Read, was with Read when she discovered O’Keefe’s body, and says the scene was immediately chaotic. But even after police left, O’Keefe’s family and friends had unanswered questions about how exactly he died. When police arrested Read on Feb. 1, their theory was that Read had accidentally hit O’Keefe with her car and was so drunk she didn’t notice. But further investigations, including a data pull from her car, revealed her back up cameras and sensors were completely functional, making an accidental strike unlikely.

Read’s case quickly caught the attention of online true-crime enthusiasts. One of the most prominent YouTubers who first posted about the case was Aidan Kearney, known as Turtleboy on YouTube. Kearney posted dozens of videos claiming Read was being set up by the Canton Police Department; popularizing alternative theories about O’Keefe’s death, including the possibility of a fight inside Albert’s home or a dog attack. (Kearney did not participate in the documentary). He often showed up to homes of O’Keefe’s friends, or confronted them in public places like sporting events. He also hosted several “Free Karen Read” rallies and protests, one of which involved marching protestors by the homes of Albert and McCabe. He was especially focused on Colin Albert, a teen who he believed might have fought with O’Keefe and accidentally killed him.

In the docuseries, Albert speaks out in a rare public interview and says Kearney’s video sent waves of targeted harassment towards him, both online and in person. “I am not the kid that these people were making me out as,” he says. “I am scared every single day about somebody coming up to me, whether they try to attack me, whether they try to do something horrible. I’m scared every day of my life for that.”

During Read’s first trial, her defense team uncovered multiple text messages from

Massachusetts State Police Trooper Michael Proctor, the lead investigator on her case, in which he referred to Read as a “bitch,” and a “wack job cunt,” adding “hopefully she kills herself.” This eroded trust in his investigation, and Proctor was fired in 2025 after months of suspension. On Nov. 10, Massachusetts State Police and Read settled a civil lawsuit alleging police misconduct during her arrest and trial.  The suit claimed the department had “an embedded culture of bigotry, misogyny, systemic failures” and cited Proctor’s text messages.

“I feel like I let down the O’Keefe family. I feel like I let down my family. I let down the state police, the DA’s office,” Proctor says in the docuseries, a rare look into his feelings on the public misstep. “I just feel like I let down so many people.”

Two jurors for Read’s second trial, identified only by their first names in the docuseries, say that Read’s case was so big that the jurors couldn’t avoid news about it before the trial. Many of the people on the jury were aware of Read’s case before they heard any testimony and several were familiar with the internet theories of a police coverup or conspiracy.

“I don’t know if it was possible to find a jury that was a completely blank slate,” Jason, a juror, says in the series.  Trending Stories Billy Ray Cyrus Shares Snapshots From Miley’s Wedding: ‘Beautiful’ to See Family ‘United by Love’ U2 Will Bring Their ‘Greatest Show on Earth’ Stadium Tour to North America in 2027 Anthem Singer Apologizes for ‘O Canada’ Flub at Leafs-Islanders Game Watch Taylor Swift Surprise Brenda Lee With ‘Rockin’ Around the Christmas Tree’ Honor

While the jury was torn on whether actual police corruption occurred, both jurors say that there were too many holes in the evidence and available testimony to prove that Read was guilty beyond a reasonable doubt. It’s a defining characteristic that not only determined Read’s fate but has continued to dog her case.

“I really did think, most likely, that she did it,” Janet, the other juror, says. “But I think by our legal system’s standards, she’s not guilty because there were so many different possibilities. He could’ve gone into the house, he could’ve gotten beaten up, he could’ve got hit by a plow. It’s almost like ‘doubt, doubt, doubt’ kinda hanging out there. It was a lot of doubt — it was more than reasonable doubt.”

In today's market, memory has become one of the most critical and expensive components in any build, and prices have only worsened thanks to the ongoing global DRAM shortage. We're keeping an eye on the volatile memory pricing to help identify any good deals that may arise.

Now, even more so than before the shortage began, price tags are fluctuating significantly from day to day, and it's becoming increasingly complex to tell when you're getting a fair deal versus overpaying for the same memory kit. We've compiled this comprehensive RAM price tracker that monitors the availability and pricing of different DDR5 and DDR4 memory kits across major U.S. retailers. Our goal is to take the guesswork out of memory shopping and help you make an informed purchasing decision.

We update this index regularly with the lowest prices available in the U.S. market for each memory kit. Our tracker covers a range of capacities and speeds across both budget and premium brands. We list the lowest price for each available memory kit, regardless of manufacturer, so you see the absolute best deals currently on offer.

The general availability of many memory kits is limited, with truly exceptional deals scarce and often selling out within seconds of appearing. High-capacity kits, particularly DDR5, frequently show the most severe stock shortages and price premiums. Therefore, timing and vigilance are especially important when shopping for memory in current times. Whether you're budget-conscious and want the best bang for your buck, or you're building a premium system and want the best RAM for gaming available, our index further below gives you the data you need to make the right purchase.

You can also check out our list of the best RAM and our RAM benchmark hierarchy to see evergreen performance data to help you make an informed decision when choosing a new GPU for your system.

Above are a few of the standout deals from the Black Friday event, which is currently taking place. For a more extensive list of Black Friday deals, head to our Black Friday RAM deals article.

DDR5 memory prices have exploded over the last year, with triple and, in some cases, quadruple increases. For instance, a conventional 32GB (2x16GB) DDR5-6000 memory kit sold for around $100 to $200 in October 2025, but the same kit now starts at $430, if it's even in stock.

Retailers are taking advantage of the DRAM shortage, just as they did with the graphics card shortage a few years ago. They've started raising DDR5 prices to align with current market conditions and sometimes bundle DDR5 memory kits with processor or motherboard purchases.

Nonetheless, these DDR5 memory kits sometimes sell for more reasonable prices. However, you have to be quick to pull the trigger, as scalpers and automated shopping bots tend to gobble up what little stock is left.

The rising prices of DDR5 memory kits have forced some consumers to stick with DDR4. However, DDR4 has also seen price hikes over the last three months, though not as drastic as DDR5. We've seen the pricing double to triple.

For reference, it was common to find 32GB (2x16GB) DDR4 memory kits selling for between $60 to $90 back in October 2025. Jump to January 2026, and the same memory kit could cost anywhere between $150 and $180, so DDR4 isn't exactly a safe haven for consumers.

We've seen more reasonably priced DDR4 memory kits on the market than DDR5. Logically, it makes little sense to build a DDR4-based system now due to processor and motherboard availability. However, if you're in desperate need of a DDR4 memory upgrade, there are viable options on the market.
